    "id": "Xecotcovach adalah dewa Maya dalam Popol Vuh.",

    "claim_ids": [
      "xecotcovach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Xecotcovach adalah dewa Maya dalam Popol Vuh. Para dewa memusnahkan manusia kayu yang tidak memiliki jiwa dan kebijaksanaan. Ia muncul bersama tiga pemusnah lainnya saat banjir.",
      "en": "Xecotcovach is a Maya deity in the Popol Vuh. The gods destroy wooden people who lack souls and wisdom. He appears with three other destroyers during the flood.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "xecotcovach-c01",
        "xecotcovach-c02",
        "xecotcovach-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Perannya adalah mencungkil mata manusia kayu. Penceritaan Spence menyebut Xecotcovach burung yang mencabut mata. Spence menempatkan pemusnahan dalam banjir Hurakan terhadap manusia kayu.",
      "en": "His role is to gouge out the wooden people’s eyes. Spence’s retelling calls Xecotcovach a bird who tears out eyes. Spence places the destruction in Hurakan’s flood against wooden people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "xecotcovach-c04",
        "xecotcovach-c05",
        "xecotcovach-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-s1",
      "published": null,

      "title": "シェコトコトヴァッチ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://sacred-texts.com/nam/pvuheng.htm",
      "title": "The Popol Vuh (Lewis Spence, 1908)",
      "publisher": "Internet Sacred Text Archive",
      "author": "Lewis Spence",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c01",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s1",
      "quote": "シェコトコヴァッチ（Xecotcovach（K'oth'owach）は、マヤ神話「ポポル・ヴフ 」や鬼神話であるグアテマラのキチェ族の文書にも登場する神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xecotcovach adalah dewa Maya dalam Popol Vuh.",
        "en": "Xecotcovach is a Maya deity in the Popol Vuh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c02",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s1",
      "quote": "原初、神々は自分達を崇め、養い、糧を用意するものを求め、木で人形を造った。だがその木の人形は魂や知恵を持たない失敗作だった。神々は失望し木の人形達を滅ぼすことにした。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para dewa memusnahkan manusia kayu yang tidak memiliki jiwa dan kebijaksanaan.",
        "en": "The gods destroy wooden people who lack souls and wisdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c03",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s1",
      "quote": "樹脂が夥しく降り注ぎ、天の心によって大洪水が引き起こされた。そしてシェコトコヴァッチ、カマロッツ、コッツバラム、トゥクムバラムが現れたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul bersama tiga pemusnah lainnya saat banjir.",
        "en": "He appears with three other destroyers during the flood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c04",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s1",
      "quote": "シェコトコヴァッチは刑を執行した責任者の1人で、木の人形達の目を抉り出したという。カマロッツは頭を切り落とし、コッツバラムは肉を食べ、トゥカンバラムは骨を砕いて小麦粉に変えてしまったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perannya adalah mencungkil mata manusia kayu.",
        "en": "His role is to gouge out the wooden people’s eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c05",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s2",
      "quote": "The bird Xecotcovach tore out their eyes; the bird Camulatz cut off their heads",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penceritaan Spence menyebut Xecotcovach burung yang mencabut mata.",
        "en": "Spence’s retelling calls Xecotcovach a bird who tears out eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xecotcovach-c06",
      "source_id": "xecotcovach-s2",
      "quote": "Then by the will of Hurakan, the Heart of Heaven, the waters were swollen, and a great flood came upon the mannikins of wood.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Spence menempatkan pemusnahan dalam banjir Hurakan terhadap manusia kayu.",
        "en": "Spence places the destruction in Hurakan’s flood against wooden people."
      }
    }
  ],

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Yamaoroshi adalah yōkai yang digambar dalam Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro karya Sekien.",
    "en": "Yamaoroshi is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "yamaoroshi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yamaoroshi adalah yōkai yang digambar dalam Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro karya Sekien. Ia bertubuh mirip manusia dan berkepala mirip parutan dengan banyak tonjolan. Peneliti menafsirkannya sebagai permainan kata Sekien atas parutan dan landak.",
      "en": "Yamaoroshi is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro. It has a humanoid body and a grater-like head covered in projections. Researchers interpret it as Sekien’s pun on a grater and porcupine.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yamaoroshi-c01",
        "yamaoroshi-c02",
        "yamaoroshi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Desainnya dibandingkan dengan sosok mirip landak dalam gulungan lebih tua. Kisah belakangan memberi parutan yang ditelantarkan tubuh dan kepala berduri. Uraian belakangan mengidentifikasi oroshi sebagai kata Jepang untuk parutan.",
      "en": "Its design is compared with porcupine-like figures in older scrolls. A later account gives the neglected grater a body and spiny head. The later account identifies oroshi as the Japanese word for grater.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yamaoroshi-c04",
        "yamaoroshi-c05",
        "yamaoroshi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c01",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s1",
      "quote": "山颪（やまおろし）は、鳥山石燕による妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yamaoroshi adalah yōkai yang digambar dalam Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro karya Sekien.",
        "en": "Yamaoroshi is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s Hyakki Tsurezure Bukuro."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c02",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s1",
      "quote": "おろし器のような頭部をした人型の妖怪として描かれ、頭にはおろし金のようなの無数の突起が並んでいる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertubuh mirip manusia dan berkepala mirip parutan dengan banyak tonjolan.",
        "en": "It has a humanoid body and a grater-like head covered in projections."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c03",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s1",
      "quote": "おろし器の表面にあるとげ状の突起を豪猪（ヤマアラシ）のとげに例え、「おろし」と「おやじ」の音の似ていることから、石燕がこの妖怪を創作したと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menafsirkannya sebagai permainan kata Sekien atas parutan dan landak.",
        "en": "Researchers interpret it as Sekien’s pun on a grater and porcupine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c04",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s1",
      "quote": "室町時代の『百鬼夜行絵巻』に描かれている浅沓を載せたヤマアラシ状の妖怪がヒントになった、あるいはトゲの生えた妖怪（画像参照）をモデルとして山颪が描かれたなどの説がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Desainnya dibandingkan dengan sosok mirip landak dalam gulungan lebih tua.",
        "en": "Its design is compared with porcupine-like figures in older scrolls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c05",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s2",
      "quote": "It sprouts a body, and the dull slicers on the grater stick out like wild spines from its head.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan memberi parutan yang ditelantarkan tubuh dan kepala berduri.",
        "en": "A later account gives the neglected grater a body and spiny head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yamaoroshi-c06",
      "source_id": "yamaoroshi-s2",
      "quote": "First, the Japanese word for grater is oroshi, which is found in this tsukumogami’s name.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan mengidentifikasi oroshi sebagai kata Jepang untuk parutan.",
        "en": "The later account identifies oroshi as the Japanese word for grater."
      }
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "yamato-no-okuni-tama-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "倭大国魂神（やまとのおおくにたまのかみ）は、日本神話に登場する神。 == 概要 == 『日本書紀』のみに登場し、他に日本大国魂神とも表記する。大和神社（奈良県天理市）の祭神として有名。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yamato no Ōkunitama adalah dewa Jepang di Kuil Ōyamato, Nara.",

      "source_id": "yamato-no-okuni-tama-no-kami-s1",

      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaisar Sujin memindahkan pemujaannya keluar istana karena takut akan kuasa ilahi.",
        "en": "Emperor Sujin moves his worship outside the palace out of fear of divine power."

      "source_id": "yamato-no-okuni-tama-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "倭大国魂は渟名城入姫命に預けて祭らせたが、髪が落ち、体が痩せて祀ることができなかった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Upaya Nunakiirihime memujanya disertai penyakit.",
        "en": "Nunakiirihime’s attempt to worship him is accompanied by illness."

    },
    {
      "id": "yamato-no-okuni-tama-no-kami-c04",
      "source_id": "yamato-no-okuni-tama-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "大国主神の別名の一つに「大国魂大神」があることから、倭大国魂神は大国主神と同神とする説がある。『大倭神社注進状』では、大己貴神（大国主神）の荒魂であるとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "id": "Yasakatome adalah dewi Shinto. Ia dipuja dalam jaringan kuil Suwa Taisha. Ia adalah istri Takeminakata dan biasanya terkait dengan Kuil Bawah Suwa.",
      "en": "Yasakatome is a Shinto goddess. She is worshipped in Suwa Taisha’s shrine network. She is Takeminakata’s wife and is usually linked with Suwa’s Lower Shrine.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yasakatome-c01",
        "yasakatome-c02",
        "yasakatome-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pangkat istana paling awal yang dicatat untuknya berasal dari 842. Ia tidak muncul dalam Kojiki atau Nihon Shoki. Pangkatnya naik bersama Takeminakata hingga peringkat kedua senior pada 867.",
      "en": "Her earliest recorded court rank dates to 842. She does not occur in the Kojiki or Nihon Shoki. Her rank rises with Takeminakata’s to senior second rank by 867.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "yasakatome-c05",
        "yasakatome-c06"

    {
      "id": "yasakatome-c04",
      "source_id": "yasakatome-s1",
      "quote": "Yasakatome's first historical attestation is in the Shoku Nihon Kōki, where the goddess is given the rank of junior fifth, lower grade (従五位下) by the imperial court in the tenth month of Jōwa 9 (842 CE), five months after the same rank was conferred on Takeminakata.",

    {
      "id": "yasakatome-c06",
      "source_id": "yasakatome-s1",
      "quote": "As Takeminakata rose up in rank, so did Yasakatome, so that by 867 CE, Yasakatome had been promoted to senior second (正二位).",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yato-no-kami adalah dewa ular Jepang yang dicatat dalam Hitachi Fudoki. Kisah itu menempatkan mereka di ladang daerah Namegata. Mereka dikaitkan dengan kami garang dan sisi alam yang ganas.",
      "en": "Yato-no-kami are Japanese snake deities recorded in the Hitachi Fudoki. The account places them in fields in Namegata county. They are associated with rough kami and the violent side of nature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yato-no-kami-c01",
        "yato-no-kami-c02",
        "yato-no-kami-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Melihat mereka dikabarkan mendatangkan kehancuran keluarga. Matachi konon membunuh dan memuja mereka pada masa Keitai. Kisah selanjutnya menyebut Maro mengusir mereka dari para pekerja pembangunan.",
      "en": "Seeing them is rumored to bring destruction to a family. Matachi is said to kill and enshrine them during Keitai’s reign. A later account has Maro drive them away from construction workers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yato-no-kami-c04",
        "yato-no-kami-c05",
        "yato-no-kami-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c01",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "The Yato-no-kami (夜刀の神; \"gods of the night-sword\") are snake deities in Japanese folklore appearing in the Hitachi no Kuni Fudoki.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yato-no-kami adalah dewa ular Jepang yang dicatat dalam Hitachi Fudoki.",
        "en": "Yato-no-kami are Japanese snake deities recorded in the Hitachi Fudoki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c02",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "They lived in Namegata county, in fields near the government office.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menempatkan mereka di ladang daerah Namegata.",
        "en": "The account places them in fields in Namegata county."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c03",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "As a snake, it was an araburu-kami (荒ぶる神; \"rough, aggressive kami\") which are fierce kami who represent the ferocious side of nature, such as raging rivers and fierce storms - as well as natural disasters.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dikaitkan dengan kami garang dan sisi alam yang ganas.",
        "en": "They are associated with rough kami and the violent side of nature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c04",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "Yato no kami were rumored to bring familial extermination on anyone who saw them.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melihat mereka dikabarkan mendatangkan kehancuran keluarga.",
        "en": "Seeing them is rumored to bring destruction to a family."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c05",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "It's told that Yato no Kami were killed and enshrined by a man named Yahazu no uji no Matachi during the time when Emperor Keitai was in reign.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matachi konon membunuh dan memuja mereka pada masa Keitai.",
        "en": "Matachi is said to kill and enshrine them during Keitai’s reign."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yato-no-kami-c06",
      "source_id": "yato-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "The Yato no kami vanished later when a man named Mibunomuroji Maro drove them away from disturbing him and his workers who were building a moat there.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah selanjutnya menyebut Maro mengusir mereka dari para pekerja pembangunan.",
        "en": "A later account has Maro drive them away from construction workers."
      }
    }
  ],

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Abura-bō adalah hantu atau api gaib Shiga dan Kyoto yang terkait dengan minyak curian. Tradisi Shiga mengidentifikasinya sebagai biksu yang mencuri minyak lampu dari Gunung Hiei.",
      "en": "Abura-bō is a ghost or supernatural fire of Shiga and Kyoto linked to stolen oil. A Shiga tradition identifies it as a monk who stole lamp oil from Mount Hiei.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abura-bo-c01",
        "abura-bo-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bunga rampai Edo juga mencatat cahaya itu di kaki barat Hiei. Di Kongōji, biksu pencuri minyak yang meninggal konon menghantui gerbang kuil.",
      "en": "An Edo miscellany also records the light at Hiei’s western foot. At Kongōji a dead oil-thieving monk is said to haunt the temple gate.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abura-bo-c03",
        "abura-bo-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "abura-bo-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%B2%B9%E5%9D%8A",
      "title": "油坊",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "abura-bo-c01",
      "source_id": "abura-bo-s1",
      "quote": "油坊（あぶらぼう）は、滋賀県や京都府に伝わる怪火、または亡霊。名称は、油を盗んだ僧侶がこれに化けたという伝承に由来する。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Abura-bō adalah hantu atau api gaib Shiga dan Kyoto yang terkait dengan minyak curian.",
        "en": "Abura-bō is a ghost or supernatural fire of Shiga and Kyoto linked to stolen oil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abura-bo-c02",
      "source_id": "abura-bo-s1",
      "quote": "滋賀県では、野洲郡欲賀村（現在は市町村合併により守山市欲賀町）に、晩春から夏にかけて油坊という怪火が現れたと伝えられており、比叡山の灯油を盗んだ僧侶が変化したものといわれた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Shiga mengidentifikasinya sebagai biksu yang mencuri minyak lampu dari Gunung Hiei.",
        "en": "A Shiga tradition identifies it as a monk who stole lamp oil from Mount Hiei."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abura-bo-c03",
      "source_id": "abura-bo-s1",
      "quote": "このような怪火は、寛保時代の雑書『諸国里人談』によれば比叡山の西麓にも現れたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bunga rampai Edo juga mencatat cahaya itu di kaki barat Hiei.",
        "en": "An Edo miscellany also records the light at Hiei’s western foot."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abura-bo-c04",
      "source_id": "abura-bo-s1",
      "quote": "こちらにも野洲郡のものと似た伝承があり、寺に灯油を届ける役目を持つ僧侶が、遊ぶ金欲しさに灯油を盗んで金を作ったが、遊びに行く前に急病で命を落としてしまい、それ以来、寺の山門に霊となって現れるようになったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Kongōji, biksu pencuri minyak yang meninggal konon menghantui gerbang kuil.",
        "en": "At Kongōji a dead oil-thieving monk is said to haunt the temple gate."
      }
    }
  ],

      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "akashaguma-c01",
      "source_id": "akashaguma-s1",
      "quote": "赤シャグマ（あかシャグマ）は、四国に伝わる妖怪。人家に住み着く赤い髪の子供のような妖怪で、座敷童子の仲間とする説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akashaguma adalah yōkai Shikoku menyerupai anak berambut merah.",
        "en": "Akashaguma is a childlike red-haired yōkai of Shikoku."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akashaguma-c02",
      "source_id": "akashaguma-s1",
      "quote": "夜に住人が寝静まった後で座敷で騒ぎ始め、台所にある食べ物を食べてしまう。 広見町（現・鬼北町）や宇和島市の伝承では小坊主（こぼうず）とも呼ばれており、山仕事に出かけた男が家に帰ってくると、薄暗い家の中、囲炉裏で数人の赤シャグマが暖をとっており、男の帰宅に気づいた赤シャグマたちは床下へと姿を消したという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Ehime menggambarkan keributan malam, makanan curian, atau sosok yang menghangatkan diri di perapian.",
        "en": "Ehime accounts describe nighttime noise, stolen food or figures warming at a hearth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akashaguma-c03",
      "source_id": "akashaguma-s1",
      "quote": "広見町（現・鬼北町）や宇和島市の伝承では小坊主（こぼうず）とも呼ばれており、山仕事に出かけた男が家に帰ってくると、薄暗い家の中、囲炉裏で数人の赤シャグマが暖をとっており、男の帰宅に気づいた赤シャグマたちは床下へと姿を消したという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah Ehime menyebut mereka menghilang di bawah lantai ketika penghuni pulang.",
        "en": "One Ehime account has them disappear under the floor when a resident returns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akashaguma-c04",
      "source_id": "akashaguma-s1",
      "quote": "かつて「化け物が出ると」と噂される古い一軒家があり、誰も住もうとしない中、ある老婆がその家を買って自宅とした。しかし夜になると噂通り赤シャグマが現れ、老婆をくすぐって悪戯した。老婆は結局、その家を立ち退いたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Tokushima menyebut perempuan tua meninggalkan rumah setelah berulang kali digelitik.",
        "en": "A Tokushima story has an old woman abandon a house after repeated tickling."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Akiha Gongen adalah dewa gunung sinkretis yang dikenal melindungi dari kebakaran. Pemujaan rakyat terhadapnya berperan dalam nama tempat Akihabara.",
      "en": "Akiha Gongen is a syncretic mountain deity renowned for protection against fire. His popular worship contributes to the place name Akihabara.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "akiha-gongen-c01",
        "akiha-gongen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Perselisihan mengakui dua pusat pemujaan Akiha. Satu tradisi menjadikan Sanshakubō petapa bersayap yang menunggangi rubah putih.",
      "en": "A dispute recognizes two centers of Akiha worship. A tradition makes Sanshakubō a winged ascetic riding a white fox.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "akiha-gongen-c03",
        "akiha-gongen-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "akiha-gongen-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%A7%8B%E8%91%89%E6%A8%A9%E7%8F%BE",
      "title": "秋葉権現",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "akiha-gongen-c01",
      "source_id": "akiha-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "秋葉権現（あきはごんげん）は秋葉山の山岳信仰と修験道が融合した神仏習合の神である。火防の霊験で広く知られ、近世期に全国に分社が勧請され秋葉講と呼ばれる講社が結成された。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akiha Gongen adalah dewa gunung sinkretis yang dikenal melindungi dari kebakaran.",
        "en": "Akiha Gongen is a syncretic mountain deity renowned for protection against fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akiha-gongen-c02",
      "source_id": "akiha-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "また、明治2年12月に相次いだ東京の大火の後に政府が建立した鎮火社（霊的な火災予防施設）においては、本来祀られていた神格を無視し民衆が秋葉権現を信仰した。その結果、周囲に置かれた延焼防止のための火除地が「秋葉ノ原」と呼ばれ、後に秋葉原という地名が誕生することになる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan rakyat terhadapnya berperan dalam nama tempat Akihabara.",
        "en": "His popular worship contributes to the place name Akihabara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akiha-gongen-c03",
      "source_id": "akiha-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "これに怒ったもう一方の本山を主張する遠州秋葉寺は訴えを起こし、江戸時代に寺社奉行において裁きが行われ、結果秋葉権現は二大霊山とすることとし、現在では信仰を広めた遠州の秋葉山本宮秋葉神社を『今の根本』、行法成就の地である越後の秋葉三尺坊大権現は『古来の根本』となった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perselisihan mengakui dua pusat pemujaan Akiha.",
        "en": "A dispute recognizes two centers of Akiha worship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "akiha-gongen-c04",
      "source_id": "akiha-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "その後、嵯峨天皇の御宇大同四己丑年、越後国蔵王堂（天台宗）十二坊の内三尺坊に住する僧、この山に来て一万座の護摩を修行し、行法に依りて翼生じ、天狗と成り、永くこの山の守護神となる。すなはち三尺坊と名乗り、白狐に乗りて飛行自在をなす。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tradisi menjadikan Sanshakubō petapa bersayap yang menunggangi rubah putih.",
        "en": "A tradition makes Sanshakubō a winged ascetic riding a white fox."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Amabiko adalah yōkai peramal yang konon muncul dari laut dalam kisah Jepang.",
    "en": "Amabiko is a prophetic yōkai said to emerge from the sea in Japanese accounts.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "amabiko-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Amabiko adalah yōkai peramal yang konon muncul dari laut dalam kisah Jepang. Teks akhir Edo dan Meiji menyebarkan gambarnya bersama laporan tertulis. Kisah meramalkan panen, wabah, dan perlindungan melalui penyalinan gambarnya.",
      "en": "Amabiko is a prophetic yōkai said to emerge from the sea in Japanese accounts. Late Edo and Meiji texts circulate its image together with written reports. Accounts predict harvests, epidemics and protection through copying its image.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amabiko-c01",
        "amabiko-c02",
        "amabiko-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ilustrasinya umumnya berambut, berwajah mirip kera, dan berkaki tiga atau empat. Kisah belakangan meramalkan panen berlimpah yang disusul bencana dan penyakit. Uraian belakangan menyebut surat kabar menyebarkan gambar sebagai jimat pelindung rumah.",
      "en": "Its illustrations commonly have hair, ape-like features and three or four legs. A later account predicts abundant harvests followed by disaster and disease. The later account describes newspapers circulating images as household protective charms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amabiko-c04",
        "amabiko-c05",
        "amabiko-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],

  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/amabiko/",
      "title": "Amabiko",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c01",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s1",
      "quote": "アマビコ（海彦、天彦、天日子、尼彦、あま彦、雨彦）、または天彦入道・尼彦入道（あまびこにゅうどう）は、肥後国熊本ほか各地に伝わる妖怪。海中などから出現をして吉凶にまつわる予言めいたことを言い残したとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Amabiko adalah yōkai peramal yang konon muncul dari laut dalam kisah Jepang.",
        "en": "Amabiko is a prophetic yōkai said to emerge from the sea in Japanese accounts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c02",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s1",
      "quote": "アマビコは、江戸時代後期から明治中期にかけての資料や新聞記事などで確認されている妖怪で、絵と文がいっしょに書かれた形式で人々の間に広まった噂話・風説として記録されたり、根拠不明あるいは時代遅れの迷信として報道されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks akhir Edo dan Meiji menyebarkan gambarnya bersama laporan tertulis.",
        "en": "Late Edo and Meiji texts circulate its image together with written reports."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c03",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s1",
      "quote": "その内容の多くは、異様な生物（3本足など）のような絵と、絵に示された存在が人間の言葉で「自分の名前」や「人間の大多数が死に絶えること」あるいは「豊作や疫病が発生すること」そして「自分の姿をかきしるした者は難をのがれることが出来ること」を告げて去ったということを記載している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah meramalkan panen, wabah, dan perlindungan melalui penyalinan gambarnya.",
        "en": "Accounts predict harvests, epidemics and protection through copying its image."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c04",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s1",
      "quote": "外見は、ほとんどが体毛におおわれた（顔が無毛、坊主頭の例あり）三本足か四足動物のようで、容貌は猿似か等とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrasinya umumnya berambut, berwajah mirip kera, dan berkaki tiga atau empat.",
        "en": "Its illustrations commonly have hair, ape-like features and three or four legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c05",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s2",
      "quote": "It foretells a period of bountiful harvest, followed by a period of disaster and disease.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan meramalkan panen berlimpah yang disusul bencana dan penyakit.",
        "en": "A later account predicts abundant harvests followed by disaster and disease."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amabiko-c06",
      "source_id": "amabiko-s2",
      "quote": "Newspapers circulated their stories, along with illustrations of the yōkai for people to hang in their homes as protective charms.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

  "claims": [

      "id": "amatsukume-c01",
      "source_id": "amatsukume-s1",
      "quote": "天津久米命（あまつくめのみこと）は、日本神話に登場する神。天槵津大来目（あめのくしつおおくめ）ともする。 == 概要 == 久米氏の祖神と記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Amatsukume adalah dewa Jepang yang dianggap leluhur klan Kume.",
        "en": "Amatsukume is a Japanese deity regarded as the Kume clan’s ancestor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amatsukume-c02",
      "source_id": "amatsukume-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では天孫降臨の場面に登場し、『日本書紀』では同場面、第四の一書に登場する。大伴氏の祖神とされる天忍日命と共に武装して邇邇芸命の先導をした。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mendampingi turunnya Ninigi sambil bersenjata bersama Ame no Oshihi.",
        "en": "He accompanies Ninigi’s descent armed alongside Ame no Oshihi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amatsukume-c03",
      "source_id": "amatsukume-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では、天津久米命と天忍日命は対等の立場で書かれているが、『日本書紀』では、天槵津大来目は天忍日命に率いられたことになっている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki memberi keduanya kedudukan setara, sedangkan Nihon Shoki menempatkannya di bawah Oshihi.",
        "en": "The Kojiki gives the two equal standing while the Nihon Shoki places him under Oshihi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amatsukume-c04",
      "source_id": "amatsukume-s1",
      "quote": "また、『日本書紀』の神武東征の場面では、天槵津大来目の子孫の久米部は、天忍日命の子孫である道臣命（ミチノオミ）の部下となっているが、『古事記』では道臣命と大久米命は対等であり、同様の相違が認められる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hubungan para keturunannya juga berbeda antara kronik.",

      }
    }

      "id": "ame-no-iwatowake-c01",
      "source_id": "ame-no-iwatowake-s1",
      "quote": "天石門別神（あまのいわとわけのかみ）は、日本神話に登場する神である。大伴連の祖神とされるが、阿波忌部・安房忌部の祖神にも同名の神が見られ、天石門別命は異なる二神を指す可能性がある（後述）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ame no Iwatowake adalah dewa Jepang yang namanya mungkin menunjuk dua sosok leluhur.",
        "en": "Ame no Iwatowake is a Japanese deity whose name may denote two ancestral figures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-iwatowake-c02",
      "source_id": "ame-no-iwatowake-s1",
      "quote": "邇邇芸命が天降る際、三種の神器に常世思金神・天手力男神・天石門別神を添えたと記され、同段で天石戸別神は又の名を櫛石窓神（くしいわまどのかみ）、豊石窓神（とよいわまどのかみ）といい、御門の神であると記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menghubungkannya dengan turunnya Ninigi serta nama Kushi-iwamado dan Toyo-iwamado.",
        "en": "The Kojiki connects him with Ninigi’s descent and the names Kushi-iwamado and Toyo-iwamado."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-iwatowake-c03",
      "source_id": "ame-no-iwatowake-s1",
      "quote": "天石門別神は古来より天皇の宮殿の四方の門に祀られていた神である。神祇官の西院では御門巫祭神として祀られて式内社とされていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan gerbang menempatkannya di pintu masuk istana kaisar.",
        "en": "Gate worship places him at imperial palace entrances."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-iwatowake-c04",
      "source_id": "ame-no-iwatowake-s1",
      "quote": "『古語拾遺』に豊磐間戸命、櫛磐間戸命の二神で神殿の御門を守ったとある。『延喜式神名帳』に御門の巫（みかむのこ）が祀る神として豊磐窓神、櫛磐窓神が記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kogo Shūi dan Engishiki mengidentifikasi dua dewa penjaga pintu suci.",
        "en": "The Kogo Shūi and Engishiki identify two gods guarding sacred entrances."
      }
    }

      "en": "Ame-no-Kaguyama is a Japanese deity also associated with the name Takakuraji. The Sendai Kuji Hongi makes him a child of Nigihayahi and an Owari ancestor.",

  "cultural_context": null,

      "title": "天香山命",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ame-no-kaguyama-c01",
      "source_id": "ame-no-kaguyama-s1",
      "quote": "天香山命（あめのかぐやまのみこと/あまの-）は、日本神話に登場する神。高倉下の別名であるという。 == 概要 == 天香語山命、天賀吾山命等とも書かれるため、「あめ（ま）のかごやまのみこと」とも読む。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ame-no-Kaguyama adalah dewa Jepang yang juga terkait dengan nama Takakuraji.",
        "en": "Ame-no-Kaguyama is a Japanese deity also associated with the name Takakuraji."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-kaguyama-c02",
      "source_id": "ame-no-kaguyama-s1",
      "quote": "『先代旧事本紀』によれば、天照太神の孫神である饒速日尊（旧事本紀では天火明命と同視する）と、天道日女命との間に生まれた神（天照太神の曾孫神）で、尾張氏等の祖神とされ、物部氏等の祖神である宇摩志摩治命（うましまぢ-）とは母神を異にする兄弟神となっている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sendai Kuji Hongi menjadikannya anak Nigihayahi dan leluhur Owari.",
        "en": "The Sendai Kuji Hongi makes him a child of Nigihayahi and an Owari ancestor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-kaguyama-c03",
      "source_id": "ame-no-kaguyama-s1",
      "quote": "『新撰姓氏録』にも見え、後裔氏族として尾張氏（左京神別等）を始め、伊福部氏（左京神別下）・六人部氏（山城神別）・津守氏（摂津神別）等を挙げている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shinsen Shōjiroku mencantumkan beberapa klan sebagai keturunannya.",
        "en": "The Shinsen Shōjiroku lists several clans descended from him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-kaguyama-c04",
      "source_id": "ame-no-kaguyama-s1",
      "quote": "また、新潟県の彌彦神社の社伝に、神武天皇の大和国平定後、勅命を受け越国を平定、開拓に従事したと伝える（詳しくは彌彦神社を参照）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Kuil Yahiko menempatkan tugas pembukaan wilayahnya di Koshi.",
        "en": "Yahiko Shrine’s tradition places his mission of settlement in Koshi."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "長白羽神（ながしらはのかみ）は『古語拾遺』の天岩戸に登場する神。思兼神に命じられ、麻を育て青和幣（あおにぎて）を織ったという。神麻続機殿神社で伊勢神宮に奉納する荒妙（あらたえ）を織った神麻続部（かんおみべ、神麻績部とも）の祖神とされ、白い衣類を白羽と呼んだのは長白羽神に由来するとされる。",

      "id": "Ame no Oshihi adalah dewa Jepang yang disebut dalam kronik. Ia adalah leluhur klan Ōtomo dan mendampingi turunnya Ninigi.",
      "en": "Ame no Oshihi is a Japanese deity named in the chronicles. He is the Ōtomo clan’s ancestor and accompanies Ninigi’s descent.",

        "ame-no-oshihi-c01",
        "ame-no-oshihi-c02"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshihi-c01",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshihi-s1",
      "quote": "アメノオシヒ（アマノオシヒ）は、記紀等に伝わる日本神話の神。 『古事記』・『日本書紀』では「天忍日命（あめのおしひのみこと）」、他文献では「天押日命」や「神狭日命」とも表記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ame no Oshihi adalah dewa Jepang yang disebut dalam kronik.",
        "en": "Ame no Oshihi is a Japanese deity named in the chronicles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshihi-c02",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshihi-s1",
      "quote": "大伴氏（大伴連/大伴宿禰）の祖神で、天孫降臨の際にニニギ（瓊瓊杵尊/邇邇芸命）に随伴したと伝わる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah leluhur klan Ōtomo dan mendampingi turunnya Ninigi.",
        "en": "He is the Ōtomo clan’s ancestor and accompanies Ninigi’s descent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshihi-c03",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshihi-s1",
      "quote": "『古語拾遺』や『先代旧事本紀』「神代本紀」では、高皇産霊尊の子とする。なお「神代本紀」では、別名に「神狭日命」の名称を挙げる。 また『新撰姓氏録』では、高皇産霊尊の五世孫または六世孫とする（後述）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi menjadikannya anak Takamimusubi atau keturunan yang lebih belakangan.",
        "en": "Traditions make him Takamimusubi’s child or a later descendant."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshihi-c04",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshihi-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』の天孫降臨（ニニギの降臨）の場面では、天忍日命・天津久米命（久米直の祖）の2人が、背に強固な靫を負い、腰に頭椎の大刀を佩き、手に天のはじ弓を持ち、 天の真鹿児矢をたばさんで、天孫の先導をしたとする。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki melengkapi dirinya dan Amatsukume dengan pedang, busur, serta panah.",
        "en": "The Kojiki equips him and Amatsukume with swords, bows and arrows."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshikumone-c01",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshikumone-s1",
      "quote": "天押雲根命（あめのおしくもねのみこと）または天押雲命（あめのおしくものみこと）は、日本神話に登場する神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ame no Oshikumone adalah dewa dalam mitologi Jepang.",
        "en": "Ame no Oshikumone is a deity in Japanese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshikumone-c02",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshikumone-s1",
      "quote": "「中臣寿詞」の伝承や摩氣神社の社伝、『大同本紀』などの史料の記述を総合すると、天孫降臨のとき地上には未熟で荒い水しか存在せず、父の命によって天押雲根命が高天原より天津水（天忍石長井水）を持ち還り、この水を皇孫に奉ったとされる。これらの伝承では天牟羅雲命の神名で登場する。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa air langit kepada keturunan kaisar saat turun ke bumi.",
        "en": "He brings heavenly water to the imperial descendant during the descent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshikumone-c03",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshikumone-s1",
      "quote": "「中臣寿詞」の伝承や摩氣神社の社伝、『大同本紀』などの史料の記述を総合すると、天孫降臨のとき地上には未熟で荒い水しか存在せず、父の命によって天押雲根命が高天原より天津水（天忍石長井水）を持ち還り、この水を皇孫に奉ったとされる。これらの伝承では天牟羅雲命の神名で登場する。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi tersebut juga menyebutnya Ame no Murakumo.",
        "en": "These traditions also call him Ame no Murakumo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ame-no-oshikumone-c04",
      "source_id": "ame-no-oshikumone-s1",
      "quote": "また旧事大成経神社本紀によると讃岐国一宮名神大社田村神社は綏靖天皇の御代(BC580年頃)、当社祭神である天隠山大神（高倉下命）と、その子の天五田根大神（天村雲命）がこの国に至り、この地の祠（現在の田村神社）の場所で国を治めた、と書かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi kuil Tamura menghubungkannya dengan memerintah suatu negeri bersama Takakuraji.",
        "en": "A Tamura shrine tradition associates him with ruling a land alongside Takakuraji."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ashite Kōjin adalah dewa tangan dan kaki yang dipuja demi pemulihan cedera dan penyakit. Pemuja mempersembahkan model tangan atau kaki, kadang gips atau kruk.",
      "en": "Ashite Kōjin is a hand-and-foot deity worshipped for recovery from injury and illness. Worshippers offer hand or foot models, and sometimes casts or crutches.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ashite-kojin-c01",
        "ashite-kojin-c02"
      ]
    },
    {

      "en": "The worship is reported in several prefectures, with Kai Shrine in Kumamoto among its sites. Some temples identify the deity with Shōmen Kongō.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "ashite-kojin-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "ashite-kojin-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%B6%B3%E6%89%8B%E8%8D%92%E7%A5%9E",
      "title": "足手荒神",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ashite-kojin-c01",
      "source_id": "ashite-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "足手荒神（あしてこうじん）は、手足の神である。民間信仰を起源とし、手足の病気や怪我に悩む者がその快癒を祈願するもので、信仰の特徴として手型や足型を奉納することが見られる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ashite Kōjin adalah dewa tangan dan kaki yang dipuja demi pemulihan cedera dan penyakit.",
        "en": "Ashite Kōjin is a hand-and-foot deity worshipped for recovery from injury and illness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ashite-kojin-c02",
      "source_id": "ashite-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "民間信仰を起源とし、手足の病気や怪我に悩む者がその快癒を祈願するもので、信仰の特徴として手型や足型を奉納することが見られる。中にはギプスや松葉杖などを奉納するものもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemuja mempersembahkan model tangan atau kaki, kadang gips atau kruk.",
        "en": "Worshippers offer hand or foot models, and sometimes casts or crutches."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ashite-kojin-c03",
      "source_id": "ashite-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "信仰される地域は、秋田県、福岡県、熊本県、大分県などで、四国に及んでいるとの報告もある。熊本県嘉島町の甲斐神社（中郡甲斐神社）、熊本県大津町の西鶴甲斐神社や、熊本県八代市の医王寺は、「足手荒神」と通称されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya dicatat di beberapa prefektur, dengan Kuil Kai di Kumamoto sebagai salah satu tempatnya.",
        "en": "The worship is reported in several prefectures, with Kai Shrine in Kumamoto among its sites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ashite-kojin-c04",
      "source_id": "ashite-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "熊本県八代市の医王寺や、熊本県阿蘇市の西巌殿寺には、足手荒神として青面金剛が祀られているという。青面金剛は、庚申信仰の本尊であり、病魔退散の力を有するとされているものである。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kuil menyamakan dewa itu dengan Shōmen Kongō.",
        "en": "Some temples identify the deity with Shōmen Kongō."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Ato-oi-kozō is a mountain-spirit yōkai of eastern Tanzawa in Kanagawa. It resembles a child wearing ragged mats, patterned clothing or fur.",

  "cultural_context": null,

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ato-oi-kozo-c01",
      "source_id": "ato-oi-kozo-s1",
      "quote": "後追い小僧（あとおいこぞう）は、神奈川県丹沢地方東部に伝わる妖怪で、丹沢の山霊（山の神霊）の一種。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ato-oi-kozō adalah yōkai roh gunung dari Tanzawa timur di Kanagawa.",
        "en": "Ato-oi-kozō is a mountain-spirit yōkai of eastern Tanzawa in Kanagawa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ato-oi-kozo-c02",
      "source_id": "ato-oi-kozo-s1",
      "quote": "姿は4歳から10歳程度の子供のようで、ときには15歳ほどのこともある。服装はぼろぼろのむしろや、絣（かすり）の着物、毛皮などを纏っている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyerupai anak yang memakai tikar lusuh, pakaian bermotif, atau bulu.",
        "en": "It resembles a child wearing ragged mats, patterned clothing or fur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ato-oi-kozo-c03",
      "source_id": "ato-oi-kozo-s1",
      "quote": "山中を人間が歩いていると、後追い小僧は無言でその者の後をつけて歩く。つけられた者が気配を感じて後ろを振り向くと、木や岩の陰に隠れ、姿を消してしまう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diam-diam mengikuti pejalan dan bersembunyi ketika mereka menoleh.",
        "en": "It silently follows walkers and hides when they turn around."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ato-oi-kozo-c04",
      "source_id": "ato-oi-kozo-s1",
      "quote": "声を出すことも物音を立てることもなく、人間に対して危害を加えることもないが、何度も後追い小僧に遭った人は、食べ物（握り飯、芋、菓子、干し柿など）を辺りの岩や切り株の上に置いて行ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menyebutnya tidak berbahaya dan menggambarkan orang meninggalkan makanan untuknya.",
        "en": "Accounts call it harmless and describe leaving food for it."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "化け銀杏の精（ばけいちょうのせい）または化け銀杏の霊（ばけいちょうのれい）は、妖怪漫画家・水木しげるの著書にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bakeichōnosei adalah yōkai Jepang dalam buku Shigeru Mizuki.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bakeichonosei-c03",
      "source_id": "bakeichonosei-s1",
      "quote": "水木は、与謝蕪村による『蕪村妖怪絵巻』にある「鎌倉若宮八幡いてう（銀杏）の木のばけ者」をもとにしてこの妖怪画を描いている。",

  "countries": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Buruburu adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Buruburu is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "buruburu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Buruburu adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Ia menempel pada kerah seseorang dan menjelaskan rasa merinding ketakutan. Nama dewa kepengecutan dan zozogami ditafsirkan melalui ketakutan dan gemetar.",
      "en": "Buruburu is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. It clings to a person’s collar and explains a fearful chill. The names cowardice god and zozogami are interpreted through fear and trembling.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buruburu-c01",
        "buruburu-c02",
        "buruburu-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah belakangan menggambarkannya sebagai roh kepengecutan. Kisah belakangan menyebutnya menempel pada kerah dan menyentuh tengkuk. Namanya dijelaskan sebagai tiruan bunyi Jepang untuk gemetar.",
      "en": "A later account describes it as a cowardice spirit. The later account has it cling to collars and touch the back of the neck. Its name is explained as Japanese sound symbolism for shivering.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buruburu-c04",
        "buruburu-c05",
        "buruburu-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "buruburu-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%9C%87%E3%80%85",
      "title": "震々",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/buruburu/",
      "title": "Buruburu",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c01",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s1",
      "quote": "震々（ぶるぶる）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔画図続百鬼』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buruburu adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Buruburu is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c02",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s1",
      "quote": "『今昔画図続百鬼』によれば、恐怖を感じた人間の首筋がぞっとするのは、この震々が人間の襟元に取り憑くためであり、別名「臆病神」「ぞぞ神」ともされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menempel pada kerah seseorang dan menjelaskan rasa merinding ketakutan.",
        "en": "It clings to a person’s collar and explains a fearful chill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c03",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s1",
      "quote": "稲田篤信らによれば、臆病神とは敵に後ろを見せる心を生じさせる神であり、ぞぞ神とは恐怖のために全身の毛をそそけ立たせる神とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dewa kepengecutan dan zozogami ditafsirkan melalui ketakutan dan gemetar.",
        "en": "The names cowardice god and zozogami are interpreted through fear and trembling."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c04",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s2",
      "quote": "Buruburu are sometimes referred to as the spirit of cowardice.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menggambarkannya sebagai roh kepengecutan.",
        "en": "A later account describes it as a cowardice spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c05",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s2",
      "quote": "They possess people by clinging to their shirt collars and touching the backs of their necks.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebutnya menempel pada kerah dan menyentuh tengkuk.",
        "en": "The later account has it cling to collars and touch the back of the neck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buruburu-c06",
      "source_id": "buruburu-s2",
      "quote": "The words buruburu and zo are Japanese onomatopoeia for the sound of shivering and the chill of fear.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dijelaskan sebagai tiruan bunyi Jepang untuk gemetar.",
        "en": "Its name is explained as Japanese sound symbolism for shivering."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Chiū-sìng, disebut Hahakigami dalam uraian yang dikutip, adalah makhluk kepercayaan rakyat Jepang.",

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chiū-sìng, disebut Hahakigami dalam uraian yang dikutip, adalah makhluk kepercayaan rakyat Jepang. Kisah itu menyebutnya tsukumogami yang ramah terhadap manusia. Sapu ritual yang diperlakukan baik memperoleh jiwa setelah satu abad.",
      "en": "Chiū-sìng, called Hahakigami in the cited account, is a being of Japanese folk belief. The account calls it a tsukumogami friendly to humans. A well-treated ritual broom gains a soul after a century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chiu-sing-c01",
        "chiu-sing-c02",
        "chiu-sing-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menyapu aula dan tangga suci dengan sendirinya. Ia bekerja hanya ketika tidak ada orang yang melihat. Sekien menyebut bermimpi tentang roh itu setelah badai musim gugur.",
      "en": "It sweeps consecrated halls and stairs on its own. It works only when nobody is watching. Sekien says he dreamed of the spirit after an autumn storm.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chiu-sing-c04",
        "chiu-sing-c05",
        "chiu-sing-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c01",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "ist der Name eines fiktiven Wesens des japanischen Volksglaubens.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chiū-sìng, disebut Hahakigami dalam uraian yang dikutip, adalah makhluk kepercayaan rakyat Jepang.",
        "en": "Chiū-sìng, called Hahakigami in the cited account, is a being of Japanese folk belief."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c02",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "Es ist einer der wenigen Tsukumogami, die dem Menschen wohlgesinnt sind.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menyebutnya tsukumogami yang ramah terhadap manusia.",
        "en": "The account calls it a tsukumogami friendly to humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c03",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "Der Hahakigami wird als Reisigbesen beschrieben, der nach Ablauf von mindestens 100 Jahren eine eigene Seele erlangt – allerdings nur, wenn er gut behandelt und nur zur rituellen Reinigung in Tempeln und Schreinen verwendet wurde.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sapu ritual yang diperlakukan baik memperoleh jiwa setelah satu abad.",
        "en": "A well-treated ritual broom gains a soul after a century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c04",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "Dann erwacht er zum Leben und beginnt, von selbst die geweihten Hallen und Treppen zu fegen.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyapu aula dan tangga suci dengan sendirinya.",
        "en": "It sweeps consecrated halls and stairs on its own."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c05",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "Er tut dies aber nur, wenn niemand hinschaut.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bekerja hanya ketika tidak ada orang yang melihat.",
        "en": "It works only when nobody is watching."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chiu-sing-c06",
      "source_id": "chiu-sing-s1",
      "quote": "Sekien merkt an, dass er nach einem heftigen Herbststurm von einem Hahakigami geträumt habe.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menyebut bermimpi tentang roh itu setelah badai musim gugur.",
        "en": "Sekien says he dreamed of the spirit after an autumn storm."
      }
    }

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cikap-kamuy adalah dewa burung hantu dan tanah dalam tradisi Ainu. Ia mengawasi perilaku manusia dan kamuy lainnya. Ia dikaitkan dengan keberhasilan materi.",
      "en": "Cikap-kamuy is an Ainu god of owls and land. He oversees the behavior of people and other kamuy. He is associated with material success.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cikap-kamuy-c01",
        "cikap-kamuy-c02",
        "cikap-kamuy-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai burung hantu besar, berbeda dari burung hantu kecil pembawa pertanda buruk. Pengawasannya atas desa memberinya peran sebagai penguasa wilayah. Sebagian kisah daerah menyebut air matanya menjadi emas dan perak.",
      "en": "He is depicted as a great owl rather than smaller ominous owls. His oversight of villages gives him the role of domain master. Some regional accounts make his tears gold and silver.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cikap-kamuy-c04",
        "cikap-kamuy-c05",
        "cikap-kamuy-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "story_mode": null,

  "claims": [

      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Cikap-kamuy (also called Kotan-kor-kamuy, which should not be confused with Kotan-kar-kamuy) is the Ainu kamuy (god) of owls and the land.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cikap-kamuy adalah dewa burung hantu dan tanah dalam tradisi Ainu.",
        "en": "Cikap-kamuy is an Ainu god of owls and land."
      }
    },
    {

      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "He is responsible for overseeing the behavior of humans and kamuy.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "id": "cikap-kamuy-c03",
      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "He is considered a deity of material success.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan keberhasilan materi.",
        "en": "He is associated with material success."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cikap-kamuy-c04",
      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Cikap-Kamuy is depicted as a great owl, as opposed to smaller owls (such as little horned owl) that represent demons and other malicious spirits.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai burung hantu besar, berbeda dari burung hantu kecil pembawa pertanda buruk.",
        "en": "He is depicted as a great owl rather than smaller ominous owls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cikap-kamuy-c05",
      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "The Ainu believed that the owl watched over the mosir (country) and local kotan (villages), so Cikap-Kamuy came to be represented as the master of the domain.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengawasannya atas desa memberinya peran sebagai penguasa wilayah.",
        "en": "His oversight of villages gives him the role of domain master."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cikap-kamuy-c06",
      "source_id": "cikap-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "In some areas, his tears were said to be gold and silver.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah daerah menyebut air matanya menjadi emas dan perak.",
        "en": "Some regional accounts make his tears gold and silver."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Eritate-goromo adalah yōkai jubah pendeta dalam kumpulan Sekien. Kerahnya yang menjuntai menjadi hidung atau paruh di samping tasbih dan pedupaan. Sekien menduga itu adalah pakaian tengu.",
      "en": "Eritate-goromo is a priestly-robe yōkai in Sekien’s collection. Its drooping collar becomes a nose or beak beside prayer beads and an incense burner. Sekien speculates that it was the garment of a tengu.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eritate-goromo-c01",
        "eritate-goromo-c02",
        "eritate-goromo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kiasannya menyebut Sōjōbō dari Kurama yang terkait dengan latihan Yoshitsune. Penggambaran belakangan menambahkan mata dan janggut pada kerah yang berubah. Kisah belakangan mengidentifikasi jubah itu sebagai kimono Sōjōbō dari Gunung Kurama.",
      "en": "The allusion names Sōjōbō of Kurama, linked with Yoshitsune’s training. A later depiction adds eyes and a beard to the transformed collar. The later account identifies the robe as Sōjōbō’s kimono from Mount Kurama.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eritate-goromo-c04",
        "eritate-goromo-c05",
        "eritate-goromo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c01",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s1",
      "quote": "襟立衣（えりたてごろも）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』で描かれている日本の妖怪で、僧侶の着る襟立衣の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Eritate-goromo adalah yōkai jubah pendeta dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
        "en": "Eritate-goromo is a priestly-robe yōkai in Sekien’s collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c02",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s1",
      "quote": "柄香炉を前に置いて数珠を持ち、本来は立てた状態で着用するはずの襟立衣の襟の部分が顔に垂れ下がり鼻かくちばしのようになった妖怪として描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kerahnya yang menjuntai menjadi hidung atau paruh di samping tasbih dan pedupaan.",
        "en": "Its drooping collar becomes a nose or beak beside prayer beads and an incense burner."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c03",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s1",
      "quote": "石燕は解説文で「くらま山の僧正坊のゑり立衣なるべしと夢心におもひぬ」と記しており、天狗の着用していた僧衣なのではないかと述べている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menduga itu adalah pakaian tengu.",
        "en": "Sekien speculates that it was the garment of a tengu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c04",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s1",
      "quote": "文中に挙げられている「鞍馬山の僧正坊」とは大天狗のひとりとして修験道や能などを通じて名前が知られている天狗の名であり、牛若丸（源義経）に軍学や剣術を教えたと語られている存在でもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kiasannya menyebut Sōjōbō dari Kurama yang terkait dengan latihan Yoshitsune.",
        "en": "The allusion names Sōjōbō of Kurama, linked with Yoshitsune’s training."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c05",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s2",
      "quote": "the long, pointed collar has transformed into a long, pointed nose, and it has sprouted eyes and a beard.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan menambahkan mata dan janggut pada kerah yang berubah.",
        "en": "A later depiction adds eyes and a beard to the transformed collar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eritate-goromo-c06",
      "source_id": "eritate-goromo-s2",
      "quote": "Eritategoromo was once the kimono which was worn by Sōjōbō, King of the Tengu, who lives on Mount Kurama, north of Kyoto.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan mengidentifikasi jubah itu sebagai kimono Sōjōbō dari Gunung Kurama.",
        "en": "The later account identifies the robe as Sōjōbō’s kimono from Mount Kurama."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fukuro-mujina adalah yōkai Jepang yang digambar Sekien.",
    "en": "Fukuro-mujina is a Japanese yōkai illustrated by Sekien.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fukuro-mujina-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fukuro-mujina adalah yōkai Jepang yang digambar Sekien. Gambarnya memperlihatkan mujina menyerupai perempuan yang membawa kantong perlengkapan tidur. Peneliti menghubungkannya dengan pepatah tentang menilai luak yang belum terlihat.",
      "en": "Fukuro-mujina is a Japanese yōkai illustrated by Sekien. Its image shows a womanlike mujina carrying a bedding bag. Researchers connect it with a proverb about valuing an unseen badger.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fukuro-mujina-c01",
        "fukuro-mujina-c02",
        "fukuro-mujina-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tafsir lain menganggap kantong itu sendiri sebagai benda yang hidup. Penggambaran belakangan menekankan kantong besar di bahunya. Uraian itu lebih mendukung kantong berhantu yang mengambil rupa mujina.",
      "en": "Another interpretation treats the bag itself as the animated object. A later depiction emphasizes the large sack over its shoulder. That account favors a haunted bag assuming a mujina’s appearance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fukuro-mujina-c04",
        "fukuro-mujina-c05",
        "fukuro-mujina-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

      "published": null,

    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-s2",
      "published": null,

      "title": "Fukuro mujina",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c01",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s1",
      "quote": "袋狢（ふくろむじな）は、鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fukuro-mujina adalah yōkai Jepang yang digambar Sekien.",
        "en": "Fukuro-mujina is a Japanese yōkai illustrated by Sekien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c02",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s1",
      "quote": "宿直袋（宿直用の布団などを入れる袋）を担いだ人間女性風のムジナの妖怪が描かれており、解説文には以下のように述べられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya memperlihatkan mujina menyerupai perempuan yang membawa kantong perlengkapan tidur.",
        "en": "Its image shows a womanlike mujina carrying a bedding bag."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c03",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s1",
      "quote": "「穴のむじなの直をする」とは、手に入れていないものの評価は難しい、との意味であり、無意味な当て推量を表した諺を指しており、袋狢はこの諺と『百鬼夜行絵巻』の妖怪とをモデルとして描かれたものと解釈されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menghubungkannya dengan pepatah tentang menilai luak yang belum terlihat.",
        "en": "Researchers connect it with a proverb about valuing an unseen badger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c04",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s1",
      "quote": "『百鬼徒然袋』にある妖怪はほとんどが付喪神などの器物の妖怪のため、この袋狢もムジナではなく袋のほうが本体だとする解釈もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir lain menganggap kantong itu sendiri sebagai benda yang hidup.",
        "en": "Another interpretation treats the bag itself as the animated object."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c05",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s2",
      "quote": "A very large sack is slung over their shoulder.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan menekankan kantong besar di bahunya.",
        "en": "A later depiction emphasizes the large sack over its shoulder."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fukuro-mujina-c06",
      "source_id": "fukuro-mujina-s2",
      "quote": "it is likely that fukuro mujina are actually haunted bags which take on the appearance of mujina, rather than mujina pretending to be humans.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian itu lebih mendukung kantong berhantu yang mengambil rupa mujina.",
        "en": "That account favors a haunted bag assuming a mujina’s appearance."
      }
    }
  ],

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Futsunomitama adalah roh pedang suci yang dipuja sebagai dewa di Isonokami. Pedang mitos itu mempunyai nama suci lain, termasuk Sajifutsu dan Mikafutsu.",
      "en": "Futsunomitama is the spirit of a sacred sword worshipped as a deity at Isonokami. The mythic sword has alternate sacred names including Sajifutsu and Mikafutsu.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "futsunomitama-c01",
        "futsunomitama-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Takemikazuchi memakai pedang itu dalam penaklukan, dan Takakuraji kemudian membawanya kepada Jinmu. Kekuatan rohaninya memulihkan pasukan Jinmu dari pengaruh beracun.",
      "en": "Takemikazuchi uses the sword in pacification, and Takakuraji later brings it to Jinmu. Its spiritual power restores Jinmu’s army from poisonous influence.",

        "futsunomitama-c04"
      ]

  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "futsunomitama-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%B8%83%E9%83%BD%E5%BE%A1%E9%AD%82",
      "title": "布都御魂",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "futsunomitama-c01",
      "source_id": "futsunomitama-s1",
      "quote": "神武の治世にあっては、物部氏、穂積氏らの祖と言われる宇摩志麻治命（うましまじのみこと）が宮中で祭ったが、崇神天皇の代に至り、同じく物部氏の伊香色雄命（いかがしこおのみこと）の手によって石上神宮に移され、御神体となる。同社の祭神である布都御魂大神（ふつのみたまのおおかみ）は、布都御魂の霊とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Futsunomitama adalah roh pedang suci yang dipuja sebagai dewa di Isonokami.",
        "en": "Futsunomitama is the spirit of a sacred sword worshipped as a deity at Isonokami."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "futsunomitama-c02",
      "source_id": "futsunomitama-s1",
      "quote": "布都御魂（ふつのみたま）は、記紀神話に現れる霊剣。韴霊剣、布都御魂剣（ふつみたまのつるぎ）とも言う。佐士布都神（さじふつのかみ）、甕布都神（みかふつのかみ）とも言う。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pedang mitos itu mempunyai nama suci lain, termasuk Sajifutsu dan Mikafutsu.",
        "en": "The mythic sword has alternate sacred names including Sajifutsu and Mikafutsu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "futsunomitama-c03",
      "source_id": "futsunomitama-s1",
      "quote": "建御雷神（たけみかずちのかみ）はこれを用い、葦原中国（あしはらのなかつくに）を平定した。神武東征の折り、ナガスネヒコ誅伐に失敗し、熊野山中で危機に陥った時、高倉下が神武天皇の下に持参した剣が布都御魂で、その剣の霊力は軍勢を毒気から覚醒させ、活力を得てのちの戦争に勝利し、大和の征服に大いに役立ったとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Takemikazuchi memakai pedang itu dalam penaklukan, dan Takakuraji kemudian membawanya kepada Jinmu.",
        "en": "Takemikazuchi uses the sword in pacification, and Takakuraji later brings it to Jinmu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "futsunomitama-c04",
      "source_id": "futsunomitama-s1",
      "quote": "神武東征の折り、ナガスネヒコ誅伐に失敗し、熊野山中で危機に陥った時、高倉下が神武天皇の下に持参した剣が布都御魂で、その剣の霊力は軍勢を毒気から覚醒させ、活力を得てのちの戦争に勝利し、大和の征服に大いに役立ったとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

        "id": "Kekuatan rohaninya memulihkan pasukan Jinmu dari pengaruh beracun.",

      "id": "Golden Kite adalah burung emas suci dalam kisah Jinmu di Nihon Shoki. Ia bertengger pada busur Jinmu dan menyilaukan pasukan lawan dengan cahayanya.",
      "en": "The Golden Kite is a sacred golden bird in the Nihon Shoki’s Jinmu narrative. It perches on Jinmu’s bow and dazzles the opposing army with its light.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "golden-kite-c01",
        "golden-kite-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia tidak muncul dalam Kojiki dan kerap tertukar dengan Yatagarasu. Tradisi kuil Kamo menganggap kedua burung itu perwujudan Kamo Taketsunumi.",
      "en": "It is absent from the Kojiki and is often confused with Yatagarasu. Kamo shrine tradition regards both birds as manifestations of Kamo Taketsunumi.",

  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "golden-kite-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%87%91%E9%B5%84",
      "title": "金鵄",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "golden-kite-c01",
      "source_id": "golden-kite-s1",
      "quote": "金鵄（きんし）は、『日本書紀』に登場し、神武天皇による日本建国を導いた金色の鵄。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Golden Kite adalah burung emas suci dalam kisah Jinmu di Nihon Shoki.",
        "en": "The Golden Kite is a sacred golden bird in the Nihon Shoki’s Jinmu narrative."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-kite-c02",
      "source_id": "golden-kite-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』の記述では、東征を進める彦火火出見（後の神武天皇）が長髄彦と戦っている際に、金色の霊鵄が天皇の弓に止まると、その体から発する光で長髄彦の軍兵たちの目がくらみ、東征軍が勝利することができたとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertengger pada busur Jinmu dan menyilaukan pasukan lawan dengan cahayanya.",
        "en": "It perches on Jinmu’s bow and dazzles the opposing army with its light."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-kite-c03",
      "source_id": "golden-kite-s1",
      "quote": "ただし、『古事記』に金鵄は登場せず、神武東征の際に熊野から大和へ東征軍を道案内した八咫烏と混同、あるいは同視されることが多い。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tidak muncul dalam Kojiki dan kerap tertukar dengan Yatagarasu.",
        "en": "It is absent from the Kojiki and is often confused with Yatagarasu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-kite-c04",
      "source_id": "golden-kite-s1",
      "quote": "また、平安時代から存続する賀茂神社においては、金鵄および八咫烏ともに、賀茂建角身命の化身とされており、この二つを合わせて「金鵄八咫烏」と呼び祀っている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi kuil Kamo menganggap kedua burung itu perwujudan Kamo Taketsunumi.",
        "en": "Kamo shrine tradition regards both birds as manifestations of Kamo Taketsunumi."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

      "id": "Gozuryū adalah naga dari danau yang dahulu ada di Kamakura, dipuja setelah bertobat. Legenda menghubungkan bencana dan kurban anak dengan kekejamannya.",

      ]

      "claim_ids": [

      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gozuryu-c01",
      "source_id": "gozuryu-s1",
      "quote": "五頭竜（ごずりゅう）は、伝承で神奈川県鎌倉市深沢に当時あった湖にすんでいたといわれる竜である。改心したあとは五頭竜大神ともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gozuryū adalah naga dari danau yang dahulu ada di Kamakura, dipuja setelah bertobat.",
        "en": "Gozuryū is a dragon of a former lake in Kamakura, worshipped after reform."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gozuryu-c02",
      "source_id": "gozuryu-s1",
      "quote": "五頭竜があらゆる天変地異を引き起こすため、これを宥めるべく、津村の長者は16人いた子供達を1人残らず五頭竜に捧げた。この地名は昔「子死越」と呼ばれていたが、それは五頭竜に捧げられた生贄の名残である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menghubungkan bencana dan kurban anak dengan kekejamannya.",
        "en": "The legend connects calamities and child sacrifices with its cruelty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gozuryu-c03",
      "source_id": "gozuryu-s1",
      "quote": "あるとき江ノ島が隆起し、そこに弁天様が住むことになった。この地域を支配する五頭竜は、弁天様に一目惚れして求婚した。しかし弁天様は、人間に対する残虐非道の行いを理由に求婚を断った。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melamar Benzaiten di Enoshima, yang menolaknya karena menyakiti manusia.",
        "en": "It proposes to Benzaiten on Enoshima, who rejects it for harming people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gozuryu-c04",
      "source_id": "gozuryu-s1",
      "quote": "五頭竜はしぶしぶ湖に帰ったが、翌日に心を改め、人間を守ることを弁天様に誓ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertobat dan berjanji melindungi manusia.",
        "en": "It reforms and promises to protect people."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Haradashi adalah yōkai Jepang yang menonjol dalam buku Satō Arifumi.",
    "en": "Haradashi is a Japanese yōkai prominent in Satō Arifumi’s books.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "haradashi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Haradashi adalah yōkai Jepang yang menonjol dalam buku Satō Arifumi. Penggambaran belakangan memberinya tarian perut yang menghibur orang yang gelisah. Peneliti mempertanyakan perilaku tersebut karena catatan lebih tua tidak membuktikannya.",
      "en": "Haradashi is a Japanese yōkai prominent in Satō Arifumi’s books. Later portrayals give it a belly dance that comforts troubled people. Researchers question these behaviors because earlier records do not substantiate them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haradashi-c01",
        "haradashi-c02",
        "haradashi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ilustrasi 1809 memperlihatkan monster berwajah di perut yang muncul dari gulungan. Kisah modern menekankan perilaku ceria dan tidak berbahaya. Kisah modern memberinya tubuh tanpa kepala dengan wajah di perut.",
      "en": "An 1809 illustration depicts a belly-faced monster emerging from a scroll. A modern account emphasizes cheerful and harmless behavior. The modern account gives it a headless torso with a face on its belly.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haradashi-c04",
        "haradashi-c05",
        "haradashi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "haradashi-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%AF%E3%82%89%E3%81%A0%E3%81%97",
      "title": "はらだし",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/haradashi/",
      "title": "Haradashi",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c01",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s1",
      "quote": "はらだし、腹出しは、大きな顔に手足がついたような姿の日本の妖怪。作家・佐藤有文の妖怪関連の書籍に多く登場している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Haradashi adalah yōkai Jepang yang menonjol dalam buku Satō Arifumi.",
        "en": "Haradashi is a Japanese yōkai prominent in Satō Arifumi’s books."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c02",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s1",
      "quote": "悩んでいる人間が酒でもてなすと、腹踊りをしてその悩みを消し去り、希望をわかせるなどとも解説されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan memberinya tarian perut yang menghibur orang yang gelisah.",
        "en": "Later portrayals give it a belly dance that comforts troubled people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c03",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s1",
      "quote": "以上のような解説は江戸時代の書籍や伝承資料などには確認されておらず、情報の出典が不明のため、その解説は佐藤有文などによる創作ではないかと平成以降（2000年～）には指摘されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti mempertanyakan perilaku tersebut karena catatan lebih tua tidak membuktikannya.",
        "en": "Researchers question these behaviors because earlier records do not substantiate them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c04",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s1",
      "quote": "粕三平の著書『お化け図会』では、1809年（文化6年）刊行の読本『浮牡丹全伝』（山東京伝著、歌川豊広画）の口絵に描かれた腹に顔を持つ妖怪（画像参照）を示し、古寺で腹に顔を作って人を脅かす「はらだしの妖怪」という解説がなされているが、同図は『浮牡丹全伝』に登場する船上山の古い寺にあった百鬼夜行絵巻の古画が現実化して登場した姿を描いたものである。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrasi 1809 memperlihatkan monster berwajah di perut yang muncul dari gulungan.",
        "en": "An 1809 illustration depicts a belly-faced monster emerging from a scroll."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c05",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s2",
      "quote": "Unlike most yōkai, haradashi do not do anything harmful. They are cheerful and agreeable yokai, and enjoy amusing others and cheering people up.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah modern menekankan perilaku ceria dan tidak berbahaya.",
        "en": "A modern account emphasizes cheerful and harmless behavior."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haradashi-c06",
      "source_id": "haradashi-s2",
      "quote": "Occasionally a haradashi will appear as a headless torso with arms, legs, and comical facial features on its belly.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah modern memberinya tubuh tanpa kepala dengan wajah di perut.",
