      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hua-she-c04",
      "source_id": "hua-she-s1",
      "quote": "化蛇も、ほかの鳥獣と同様に『山海経』の本文や注に基づいて絵が描かれているのだが、大きく分けて二種類の図柄にわかれている。一つは蛇に翼の生えたもの（図参照）であるが、もう一方の明の時代の例には本文にさらに即した形状（人面の獣に翼が生え、長い尾がある）の絵もあり、そちらも後の時代まで化蛇の図として描き継がれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrasi belakangan berbeda antara ular bersayap dan binatang bersayap berwajah manusia.",
        "en": "Later illustrations differ between a winged serpent and a winged human-faced beast."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Huri adalah burung raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Ainu.",
    "en": "Huri is a giant bird in Ainu folklore.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "huri-kamuy-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Huri adalah burung raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Ainu. Namanya mengarah pada burung merah, dan sebagian kisah menggambarkan sayap sangat besar. Satu asal-usul menyebut leluhurnya yang tinggal di gua mendatangkan badai angin merusak.",
      "en": "Huri is a giant bird in Ainu folklore. Its name suggests a red bird, and some accounts describe an enormous wing. One origin makes its cave-dwelling ancestors bring destructive windstorms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huri-kamuy-c01",
        "huri-kamuy-c02",
        "huri-kamuy-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian kisah daerah menggambarkan burung tidak berbahaya yang memburu hewan besar dan paus. Kisah daerah lainnya menggambarkannya sebagai pembunuh yang perlu dibasmi. Tradisi Abashiri memakai dua pedang ipetam untuk mengalahkannya.",
      "en": "Some regional accounts describe a harmless bird hunting large game and whales. Other regional accounts portray it as a killer requiring extermination. An Abashiri tradition uses two ipetam swords to defeat it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huri-kamuy-c04",
        "huri-kamuy-c05",
        "huri-kamuy-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

  "sources": [

      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huri_(Ainu_legendary_bird)",
      "title": "Huri (Ainu legendary bird)",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c01",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "A huri or furi is a giant bird in Ainu folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huri adalah burung raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Ainu.",
        "en": "Huri is a giant bird in Ainu folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c02",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Its name suggests a red or reddish bird, and by some lore said to have a single wing measuring 28 kilometres (17 mi).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya mengarah pada burung merah, dan sebagian kisah menggambarkan sayap sangat besar.",
        "en": "Its name suggests a red bird, and some accounts describe an enormous wing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c03",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "According to an etiological tale, its ancestors had been banished to hiding in caves by the gods who sided with humans, and were vengeful, bringing wind-storm disaster to humans(§ Kunnui River).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu asal-usul menyebut leluhurnya yang tinggal di gua mendatangkan badai angin merusak.",
        "en": "One origin makes its cave-dwelling ancestors bring destructive windstorms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c04",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Some regions remember the huri as harmless to humans when it lived near them, though it hunted large game and even whales, but was offended by a girl's transgression in its drinking spot and moved away (§ Tokachi).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah daerah menggambarkan burung tidak berbahaya yang memburu hewan besar dan paus.",
        "en": "Some regional accounts describe a harmless bird hunting large game and whales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c05",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Other regions characterize the huri as man-killers, requiring eradication.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah daerah lainnya menggambarkannya sebagai pembunuh yang perlu dibasmi.",
        "en": "Other regional accounts portray it as a killer requiring extermination."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huri-kamuy-c06",
      "source_id": "huri-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "The eradication was done with the use of two special ipetam swords in the lore of § Abashiri (and Bihoro).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Abashiri memakai dua pedang ipetam untuk mengalahkannya.",
        "en": "An Abashiri tradition uses two ipetam swords to defeat it."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "五十猛神（イタケルノミコト / イソタケルノミコト）は、日本神話に登場する神。イザナギ・イザナミの子であるスサノオの子で、オオヤツヒメ・ツマツヒメ（大屋津姫命、枛津姫命）は妹。",

      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "id": "isotakeru-c04",
      "source_id": "isotakeru-s1",
      "quote": "五十猛神が天降る際に多くの樹木の種を持っていたが、新羅には植えずに全てを持ってきて、九州からはじめて大八洲国に植えたので、青山に被われる国となったという。",

    "en": "Iwakamutsukari is a legendary ancient Japanese figure recorded in the Nihon Shoki.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "iwakamutsukari-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iwakamutsukari adalah sosok Jepang kuno legendaris yang dicatat dalam Nihon Shoki. Ia adalah leluhur garis keturunan dan kini dipuja sebagai dewa memasak.",
      "en": "Iwakamutsukari is a legendary ancient Japanese figure recorded in the Nihon Shoki. He is a lineage ancestor and is now worshipped as a cooking deity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iwakamutsukari-c01",
        "iwakamutsukari-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu kisah menyebutnya menyiapkan kerang bagi Kaisar Keikō dan menerima kelompok pelayan turun-temurun. Tradisi lain menggambarkan beberapa hidangan dan perintah kaisar untuk terus memasok makanan.",
      "en": "One narrative has him prepare clams for Emperor Keikō and receive a hereditary service group. Another tradition describes several dishes and an imperial command to supply food permanently.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iwakamutsukari-c03",
        "iwakamutsukari-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "iwakamutsukari-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%A3%90%E9%B9%BF%E5%85%AD%E9%B4%88",
      "title": "磐鹿六鴈",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "iwakamutsukari-c01",
      "source_id": "iwakamutsukari-s1",
      "quote": "磐鹿六鴈（いわかむつかり）は、『日本書紀』等に伝わる古代日本の人物。 『日本書紀』では「磐鹿六鴈」や「六鴈臣（むつかりのおみ）」、他文献では「磐鹿六獦命」や「磐鹿六雁命」・「伊波我牟都加利命」とも表記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iwakamutsukari adalah sosok Jepang kuno legendaris yang dicatat dalam Nihon Shoki.",
        "en": "Iwakamutsukari is a legendary ancient Japanese figure recorded in the Nihon Shoki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwakamutsukari-c02",
      "source_id": "iwakamutsukari-s1",
      "quote": "第8代孝元天皇皇子の大彦命の孫で、比古伊那許志別命（大稲腰命）の子とされる。また膳臣（かしわでのおみ、膳氏：のち高橋氏）の遠祖とされるほか、現在では料理神としても信仰される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah leluhur garis keturunan dan kini dipuja sebagai dewa memasak.",
        "en": "He is a lineage ancestor and is now worshipped as a cooking deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwakamutsukari-c03",
      "source_id": "iwakamutsukari-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』の景行天皇53年10月条によれば、同天皇（第12代）が東国巡幸において上総国に至り海路から淡水門（あわのみなと：安房の水門）を渡る際、覚賀鳥（かくがのとり：ミサゴの古名）の声が聞こえたので、天皇がその姿を見ようと海の中に入ると、白蛤（うむぎ：ハマグリ）を得た。この時に磐鹿六鴈が、蒲を襷としてその白蛤を膾にして献上した。その功で六鴈は膳大伴部（かしわでのおおともべ）を賜ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah menyebutnya menyiapkan kerang bagi Kaisar Keikō dan menerima kelompok pelayan turun-temurun.",
        "en": "One narrative has him prepare clams for Emperor Keikō and receive a hereditary service group."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwakamutsukari-c04",
      "source_id": "iwakamutsukari-s1",
      "quote": "そこで六獦命は无邪志国造上祖の大多毛比、知々夫国造上祖の天上腹と天下腹らを呼び寄せ、膾・煮物・焼物を作って献上した。天皇はこれを誉め、永く御食を供進するように命じ、また六獦命に大刀を授けるとともに大伴部を与えた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi lain menggambarkan beberapa hidangan dan perintah kaisar untuk terus memasok makanan.",
        "en": "Another tradition describes several dishes and an imperial command to supply food permanently."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Iyaya adalah yōkai Jepang dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Iyaya is a Japanese yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "iyaya-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iyaya adalah yōkai Jepang dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Gambar memperlihatkan perempuan cantik dari belakang dengan wajah tua terpantul di air. Karya seni belakangan memakai nama Iyami untuk sosok serupa.",
      "en": "Iyaya is a Japanese yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. The image shows a beautiful woman from behind with an old face reflected in water. Later artworks use the name Iyami for a similar figure.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iyaya-c01",
        "iyaya-c02",
        "iyaya-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cerita Sendai belakangan mungkin ciptaan baru atau tradisi yang dihiasi. Kisah belakangan memberinya tampilan perempuan muda berpakaian indah dari belakang. Kisah belakangan itu menempatkan kejutan darinya di jalan kota pada malam hari.",
      "en": "A later Sendai narrative may be an invention or embellished tradition. A later account gives it the appearance of a well-dressed young woman from behind. That later account places its surprises on town streets at night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iyaya-c04",
        "iyaya-c05",
        "iyaya-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {
      "id": "iyaya-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%90%A6%E5%93%89",
      "title": "否哉",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/iyaya/",
      "title": "Iyaya",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c01",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s1",
      "quote": "否哉（いやや）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔百鬼拾遺』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iyaya adalah yōkai Jepang dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Iyaya is a Japanese yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c02",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s1",
      "quote": "後ろ姿は美しい女性で、水面に老人のような顔が映った姿として描かれており、解説文には「むかし 漢の東方朔 あやしき虫をみて怪哉（かいさい）と名づけしためしあり 今この否哉もこれにならひて名付たるなるべし」と、中国の東方朔の故事にある虫「怪哉」に倣って「否哉」を名づけたことが述べられているが、否哉自体の具体的な特徴などは特に書かれていない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambar memperlihatkan perempuan cantik dari belakang dengan wajah tua terpantul di air.",
        "en": "The image shows a beautiful woman from behind with an old face reflected in water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c03",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s1",
      "quote": "明治時代の絵師・鍋田玉英による『怪物画本』では、名称は「いやミ」とされており、民俗学者・藤沢衛彦の『妖怪画談全集 日本篇 上』においても、この石燕の否哉を模写したらしい妖怪が「異爺味」（いやみ）の名で紹介されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karya seni belakangan memakai nama Iyami untuk sosok serupa.",
        "en": "Later artworks use the name Iyami for a similar figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c04",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s1",
      "quote": "また山田野理夫の『東北怪談の旅』では「イヤミ」と題し、仙台の城下町に現れた妖怪が、後ろ姿は美女のようだが、振り返ると皺だらけの老人だったとする話があるが、村上はこれを石燕の否哉をもとにした創作、もしくはもともとあった伝承に否哉のイメージを加味して脚色したものと推測している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita Sendai belakangan mungkin ciptaan baru atau tradisi yang dihiasi.",
        "en": "A later Sendai narrative may be an invention or embellished tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c05",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s2",
      "quote": "From the back, iyaya look like attractive young women wearing beautiful clothing.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan memberinya tampilan perempuan muda berpakaian indah dari belakang.",
        "en": "A later account gives it the appearance of a well-dressed young woman from behind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iyaya-c06",
      "source_id": "iyaya-s2",
      "quote": "They prefer towns and roads at night where they are more likely to surprise lone travelers.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan itu menempatkan kejutan darinya di jalan kota pada malam hari.",
        "en": "That later account places its surprises on town streets at night."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Izuna Gongen is a syncretic deity associated with Mount Izuna in Shinano. Images commonly show a crow tengu on a white fox carrying a sword and cord.",

        "izuna-gongen-c01",
        "izuna-gongen-c02"

  "claims": [
    {

      "source_id": "izuna-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "飯縄権現（いづなごんげん、いいづなごんげん）とは、信濃国上水内郡（現：長野県）の飯縄山（飯綱山）に対する山岳信仰が発祥と考えられる神仏習合の神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Izuna Gongen adalah dewa sinkretis yang terkait dengan Gunung Izuna di Shinano.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izuna-gongen-c02",
      "source_id": "izuna-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "多くの場合、剣と索を持つ烏天狗が白狐に乗った姿で表され、五体、あるいは白狐には蛇が巻きつくことがある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambar umumnya memperlihatkan tengu gagak di atas rubah putih yang membawa pedang dan tali.",
        "en": "Images commonly show a crow tengu on a white fox carrying a sword and cord."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izuna-gongen-c03",
      "source_id": "izuna-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "一般に戦勝の神とされ、細川政元、九条稙通、上杉謙信、武田信玄など中世の武将たちの間で盛んに信仰された。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prajurit abad pertengahan memujanya demi kemenangan.",
        "en": "Medieval warriors worshipped him for victory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izuna-gongen-c04",
      "source_id": "izuna-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "本地仏は不動明王、荼枳尼天とされることが多いが、信州飯縄山では地蔵菩薩とされる。大日如来とされる場合もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan Buddhanya beragam antara Fudō, Dakini, Jizō, dan Dainichi.",
        "en": "His Buddhist counterpart varies among Fudō, Dakini, Jizō and Dainichi."
      }

  "long_description": [

      "id": "Izunome adalah dewi dalam Kojiki. Ia tidak muncul dalam Nihon Shoki dan hanya sedikit diceritakan tersendiri dalam Kojiki.",
      "en": "Izunome is a goddess in the Kojiki. She is absent from the Nihon Shoki and has little independent narrative in the Kojiki.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "izunome-c01",
        "izunome-c02"
      ]
    },
    {

      "en": "She appears with rectifying deities after Izanagi returns polluted from Yomi. A Karahino Shrine tradition includes her worship.",
      "claim_ids": [

      ]

      "title": "伊豆能売",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "izunome-c01",
      "source_id": "izunome-s1",
      "quote": "伊豆能売（イズノメ、歴史的仮名遣：イヅノメ）は、『古事記』に登場する女神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Izunome adalah dewi dalam Kojiki.",
        "en": "Izunome is a goddess in the Kojiki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izunome-c02",
      "source_id": "izunome-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』にのみ登場し、『日本書紀』には登場しない。『古事記』でも出自や事跡についての記述が一切ない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tidak muncul dalam Nihon Shoki dan hanya sedikit diceritakan tersendiri dalam Kojiki.",
        "en": "She is absent from the Nihon Shoki and has little independent narrative in the Kojiki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izunome-c03",
      "source_id": "izunome-s1",
      "quote": "神産みにおいて伊邪那岐命が黄泉から帰って来た際、黄泉の穢れから禍津日神2柱（大禍津日神、八十禍津日神）が生まれた。その禍津日神がもたらす禍（災厄）を直すために、直毘神2柱（神直毘神、大直毘神）と伊豆能売が生まれたとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul bersama dewa pemulih setelah Izanagi kembali tercemar dari Yomi.",
        "en": "She appears with rectifying deities after Izanagi returns polluted from Yomi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "izunome-c04",
      "source_id": "izunome-s1",
      "quote": "しかし、伊豆能売の名を冠しない式内社は現存しており、三重県津市の元伊勢伝承地の一つである「加良比乃神社」は倭姫命が天照大御神を奉戴して「片樋宮」を建立した跡地に「御倉板舉神」と「伊豆能賣神」を祭祀したのが起源とされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Kuil Karahino mencakup pemujaannya.",
        "en": "A Karahino Shrine tradition includes her worship."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jatai adalah yōkai sabuk obi yang digambar Sekien.",
    "en": "Jatai is an obi-sash yōkai illustrated by Sekien.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jatai-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jatai adalah yōkai sabuk obi yang digambar Sekien. Kepercayaan rakyat menghubungkan tidur di samping obi dengan mimpi ular. Peneliti menafsirkan sosok itu melalui permainan kata hati jahat dan tubuh ular.",
      "en": "Jatai is an obi-sash yōkai illustrated by Sekien. A folk belief connects sleeping beside an obi with dreams of snakes. Researchers interpret the figure through wordplay on a wicked heart and serpent body.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jatai-c01",
        "jatai-c02",
        "jatai-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Buku belakangan mengubah kecemburuan dalam obi menjadi ular yang menyerang laki-laki. Kisah belakangan menggambarkan sabuk yang bergerak seperti ular besar pada malam hari. Kisah belakangan menambahkan serangan pada laki-laki yang tidur.",
      "en": "Later books turn jealousy in the obi into a snake that attacks a man. A later account describes a sash moving like a large snake at night. The later account adds attacks on sleeping men.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jatai-c04",
        "jatai-c05",
        "jatai-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "jatai-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%9B%87%E5%B8%AF",
      "title": "蛇帯",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/jatai/",
      "title": "Jatai",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jatai-c01",
      "source_id": "jatai-s1",
      "quote": "蛇帯（じゃたい）は、鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『今昔百鬼拾遺』で描かれている日本の妖怪の一つで、帯の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jatai adalah yōkai sabuk obi yang digambar Sekien.",
        "en": "Jatai is an obi-sash yōkai illustrated by Sekien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-c02",
      "source_id": "jatai-s1",
      "quote": "「博物志に云」以降は中国の晋代の民俗風物誌『博物志』からの引用として、「人帯を敷いて眠ると蛇の夢を見る」と述べられており、日本でも愛媛県などの俗信で、枕元に帯を置いて寝ると蛇の夢を見るといわれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan rakyat menghubungkan tidur di samping obi dengan mimpi ular.",
        "en": "A folk belief connects sleeping beside an obi with dreams of snakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-c03",
      "source_id": "jatai-s1",
      "quote": "現在ではこの妖怪画は、「邪心（じゃしん）」と「蛇身（じゃしん）」の語呂合わせで描かれたものと解釈されており、同様に邪心と蛇身を掛けて創作された妖怪に、同じく『今昔百鬼拾遺』に収録されている布の妖怪「機尋」がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menafsirkan sosok itu melalui permainan kata hati jahat dan tubuh ular.",
        "en": "Researchers interpret the figure through wordplay on a wicked heart and serpent body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-c04",
      "source_id": "jatai-s1",
      "quote": "また近年の妖怪関連の文献では、女の嫉妬心が帯に取り憑いて蛇となり、相手の男を絞め殺そうとするものなどと解説されていることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buku belakangan mengubah kecemburuan dalam obi menjadi ular yang menyerang laki-laki.",
        "en": "Later books turn jealousy in the obi into a snake that attacks a man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-c05",
      "source_id": "jatai-s2",
      "quote": "The jatai is an animated kimono sash that slithers around like a giant snake during the night.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menggambarkan sabuk yang bergerak seperti ular besar pada malam hari.",
        "en": "A later account describes a sash moving like a large snake at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatai-c06",
      "source_id": "jatai-s2",
      "quote": "The jatai hunts after men, strangling them in their sleep.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menambahkan serangan pada laki-laki yang tidur.",
        "en": "The later account adds attacks on sleeping men."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}

    "en": "Jīvajīvaka is a Buddhist bird with two heads sharing one body.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jivajivaka-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jīvajīvaka adalah burung Buddha dengan dua kepala yang berbagi satu tubuh. Sutra Amitabha menempatkannya di Tanah Suci dan menggambarkan bulu serta nyanyian indah.",
      "en": "Jīvajīvaka is a Buddhist bird with two heads sharing one body. The Amitabha Sutra places it in the Pure Land and describes beautiful feathers and song.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jivajivaka-c01",
        "jivajivaka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu kisah menyebut satu kepala meracuni yang lain sehingga keduanya mati karena berbagi tubuh. Nyanyiannya kemudian mengajarkan bahwa menghancurkan pihak lain juga menghancurkan diri sendiri.",
      "en": "A tale says one head poisons the other, killing both because they share a body. Its song thereafter teaches that destroying others also destroys oneself.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jivajivaka-c03",
        "jivajivaka-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "jivajivaka-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%85%B1%E5%91%BD%E9%B3%A5",
      "title": "共命鳥",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jivajivaka-c01",
      "source_id": "jivajivaka-s1",
      "quote": "共命鳥（ぐみょうちょう、jīvaṃjīvaka）は、体は一つ、頭が二つある鳥で、仏教における想像上の生物。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jīvajīvaka adalah burung Buddha dengan dua kepala yang berbagi satu tubuh.",
        "en": "Jīvajīvaka is a Buddhist bird with two heads sharing one body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jivajivaka-c02",
      "source_id": "jivajivaka-s1",
      "quote": "『阿弥陀経』では、迦陵頻伽とともに極楽浄土に住むとされる。美しい羽根を持ち、妙なる声で囀ると伝えられている。命命鳥（めいめいちょう、みょうみょうちょう）・耆婆耆婆（ぎばぎば）とも呼ばれる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sutra Amitabha menempatkannya di Tanah Suci dan menggambarkan bulu serta nyanyian indah.",
        "en": "The Amitabha Sutra places it in the Pure Land and describes beautiful feathers and song."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jivajivaka-c03",
      "source_id": "jivajivaka-s1",
      "quote": "共命鳥は他の鳥と比べても殊更に美しい羽と声を持っていたが、両頭どちらも自らが世界一美しいと譲らず、ついに「片方を殺してしまえば自分が世界一になる。」と考えるに至る。そして、密かに片方の食事に毒を盛り食べさせた。結果として食べた側を殺すことは出来たものの、そもそもの身体は一つであるため食べさせた側にも毒が回り、ついに共命鳥は命を落とす。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah menyebut satu kepala meracuni yang lain sehingga keduanya mati karena berbagi tubuh.",
        "en": "A tale says one head poisons the other, killing both because they share a body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jivajivaka-c04",
      "source_id": "jivajivaka-s1",
      "quote": "このことがあってから、浄土の共命鳥は「他を滅ぼす道は己を滅ぼす道、他を生かす道こそ己の生かされる道」と鳴き続けているという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyanyiannya kemudian mengajarkan bahwa menghancurkan pihak lain juga menghancurkan diri sendiri.",
        "en": "Its song thereafter teaches that destroying others also destroys oneself."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Jūzenji adalah dewa Jepang yang terkait dengan Hiyoshi Taisha dan Enryakuji. Ia adalah salah satu dari tujuh perwujudan utama Sannō Gongen. Uraian menggambarkannya sebagai pembuat mukjizat yang membantu sekaligus dewa penghukum.",
      "en": "Jūzenji is a Japanese deity associated with Hiyoshi Taisha and Enryakuji. He is one of seven principal manifestations of Sannō Gongen. The account presents him as both a helpful miracle worker and a punishing deity.",

        "juzenji-c01",
        "juzenji-c02",
        "juzenji-c03"
      ]

    {

      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jūzenji adalah dewa Jepang yang terkait dengan Hiyoshi Taisha dan Enryakuji.",
        "en": "Jūzenji is a Japanese deity associated with Hiyoshi Taisha and Enryakuji."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "juzenji-c02",
      "source_id": "juzenji-s1",
      "quote": "He was worshipped as one of the seven key emanations of Sanno Gongen.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah salah satu dari tujuh perwujudan utama Sannō Gongen.",
        "en": "He is one of seven principal manifestations of Sannō Gongen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "juzenji-c03",
      "source_id": "juzenji-s1",
      "quote": "This powerful potent god was a benevolent miracle-worker, but at the same time, he was also an adorcist and mediumistic god, a malefic and wild god, a judge, a terrifying god who punishes and curses those who sin or do not properly worship him.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian menggambarkannya sebagai pembuat mukjizat yang membantu sekaligus dewa penghukum.",
        "en": "The account presents him as both a helpful miracle worker and a punishing deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "juzenji-c04",
      "source_id": "juzenji-s1",
      "quote": "He was a god who conveyed oracles, not only appearing in people's dreams, but also possessing children and delivering oracles through them.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyampaikan ramalan melalui mimpi dan perasukan anak.",
        "en": "He gives oracles through dreams and possession of children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "juzenji-c05",
      "source_id": "juzenji-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed that He, being a divine child (Mikogami), acted as an agent to carry out punishments and curses in order to convey the will of his parent, Ōmiya.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertindak sebagai anak ilahi yang menyampaikan kehendak Ōmiya.",
        "en": "He acts as a divine child conveying Ōmiya’s will."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "juzenji-c06",
      "source_id": "juzenji-s1",
      "quote": "He came to be identified with Ninigi no Mikoto, the grandson of Amaterasu Ōmikami.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kemudian disamakan dengan Ninigi.",

      "en": "Kage-onna is a female-shaped yōkai in Sekien’s collection. Moonlight casts a woman’s shadow on a haunted house’s paper door. Yamada Norio includes a later Yamagata story under this name.",

        "kage-onna-c02",
        "kage-onna-c03"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah itu seorang perempuan muncul di taman tetapi tidak mendekati rumah. Kisah belakangan menekankan bayangan tanpa orang terlihat yang menimbulkannya. Kisah itu menggambarkan keheningan tanpa interaksi dengan penghuni.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kage-onna adalah yōkai berbentuk perempuan dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
        "en": "Kage-onna is a female-shaped yōkai in Sekien’s collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kage-onna-c02",
      "source_id": "kage-onna-s1",
      "quote": "石燕の解説文によれば、物の怪のいる家で、月影に照らされた女の姿の影が家の障子に映るものとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cahaya bulan memantulkan bayangan perempuan pada pintu kertas rumah berhantu.",
        "en": "Moonlight casts a woman’s shadow on a haunted house’s paper door."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kage-onna-c03",
      "source_id": "kage-onna-s1",
      "quote": "伝記作家・山田野理夫の著書『東北怪談の旅』には「影女」と題し、山形県の怪談が以下のように述べられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yamada Norio memuat kisah Yamagata belakangan dengan nama ini.",
        "en": "Yamada Norio includes a later Yamagata story under this name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kage-onna-c04",
      "source_id": "kage-onna-s1",
      "quote": "話している内に今度は庭に女の姿が現れたが、家の中には近づいて来なかった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah itu seorang perempuan muncul di taman tetapi tidak mendekati rumah.",
        "en": "In that story a woman appears in the garden but does not approach the house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kage-onna-c05",
      "source_id": "kage-onna-s2",
      "quote": "Kage onna are the shadows of women projected onto windows and doors when there is no one around to cast them.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menekankan bayangan tanpa orang terlihat yang menimbulkannya.",
        "en": "A later account emphasizes shadows with no visible person casting them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kage-onna-c06",
      "source_id": "kage-onna-s2",
      "quote": "Kage onna make no sound, nor do they interact with the house or its inhabitants.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menggambarkan keheningan tanpa interaksi dengan penghuni.",
        "en": "That account describes silence and no interaction with residents."
      }
    }
  ],

  },
  "countries": null,

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kamunaobi dan Ōnaobi adalah dewa pemulih Shinto yang terkait dengan penghapusan pencemaran.",
    "en": "Kamunaobi and Ōnaobi are Shinto rectifying deities associated with removing pollution.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kamunaobi dan Ōnaobi adalah dewa pemulih Shinto yang terkait dengan penghapusan pencemaran. Mereka muncul untuk memulihkan malapetaka setelah penyucian Izanagi dari Yomi. Kojiki mencantumkan pasangan itu bersama Izunome.",
      "en": "Kamunaobi and Ōnaobi are Shinto rectifying deities associated with removing pollution. They arise to repair calamities following Izanagi’s purification from Yomi. The Kojiki lists the pair together with Izunome.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c01",
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c02",
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu versi Nihon Shoki menempatkan Ōnaobi sebelum dewa malapetaka. Terjemahan universitas menyebut kedua dewa itu pemulih malapetaka. Catatan terjemahan menjelaskan nao sebagai lurus atau memulihkan.",
      "en": "A Nihon Shoki variant places Ōnaobi before the calamity deity. The university translation presents both deities as rectifiers of calamity. The translation notes explain nao as straight or rectifying.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c04",
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c05",
        "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%9B%B4%E6%AF%98%E7%A5%9E",
      "title": "直毘神",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://kojiki.kokugakuin.ac.jp/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/13-Studies-on-the-Kojiki-Chapter-13-Ablutions-2.pdf",
      "title": "kojiki_ablutions2",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c01",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s1",
      "quote": "直毘神（なおびのかみ、なほびのかみ）は神道の神である。 穢れを払い、禍（まが）を直す神とする説がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamunaobi dan Ōnaobi adalah dewa pemulih Shinto yang terkait dengan penghapusan pencemaran.",
        "en": "Kamunaobi and Ōnaobi are Shinto rectifying deities associated with removing pollution."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c02",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s1",
      "quote": "日本神話の神産みにおいて、黄泉から帰ったイザナギが禊を行って黄泉の穢れを祓ったときに、その穢れから禍津日神が生まれた。この禍津日神がもたらす禍を直すために生まれたのが直毘神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka muncul untuk memulihkan malapetaka setelah penyucian Izanagi dari Yomi.",
        "en": "They arise to repair calamities following Izanagi’s purification from Yomi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c03",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では八十禍津日神・大禍津日神が成った後に神直毘神（かみなほびのかみ）、大直毘神（おほなほびのかみ）と伊豆能売の三柱が成ったとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki mencantumkan pasangan itu bersama Izunome.",
        "en": "The Kojiki lists the pair together with Izunome."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c04",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s1",
      "quote": "同段第十の一書では少し異なっており、イザナギが禊の際に大直日神を生み、その後に大綾津日神（大禍津日神と同一神格）を生んだとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu versi Nihon Shoki menempatkan Ōnaobi sebelum dewa malapetaka.",
        "en": "A Nihon Shoki variant places Ōnaobi before the calamity deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c05",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s2",
      "quote": "Next, to rectify the calamities [they brought], a deity named Kamunaobi no kami 神直毗神 came into existence. Next, Ōnaobi no kami 大直毗神 (2).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan universitas menyebut kedua dewa itu pemulih malapetaka.",
        "en": "The university translation presents both deities as rectifiers of calamity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-c06",
      "source_id": "kamunaobi-and-onaobi-s2",
      "quote": "These nao (“straight,” “rectifying”) deities are the counterpart to the maga deities described in the previous text note.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan terjemahan menjelaskan nao sebagai lurus atau memulihkan.",
        "en": "The translation notes explain nao as straight or rectifying."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kanayago adalah dewa pandai besi di wilayah Chūgoku Jepang, biasanya perempuan tetapi kadang laki-laki. Lukisan kuil menggambarkan dewa itu sebagai Sanbō Kōjin sinkretis.",
      "en": "Kanayago is a deity of smiths in Japan’s Chūgoku region, usually female but sometimes male. A shrine painting portrays the deity as syncretic Sanbō Kōjin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kanayago-kami-c01",
        "kanayago-kami-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda kuil menyebut dewa itu tiba di Izumo dengan bangau putih. Kisah setempat menyebut dewa itu mengajarkan pandai besi.",

      "claim_ids": [

        "kanayago-kami-c04"

  "cultural_context": null,

      "title": "金屋子神",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kanayago-kami-c01",
      "source_id": "kanayago-kami-s1",
      "quote": "金屋子神（かなやごかみ、かなやこかみ、等）とは、中国地方を中心に鍛冶屋に信仰される神。一般には女神であるとされるが、男神とする説もある。金山彦・金山媛や天目一箇神と同一、もしくは何らかの関係がある神とされるが、全く別神とする説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kanayago adalah dewa pandai besi di wilayah Chūgoku Jepang, biasanya perempuan tetapi kadang laki-laki.",
        "en": "Kanayago is a deity of smiths in Japan’s Chūgoku region, usually female but sometimes male."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanayago-kami-c02",
      "source_id": "kanayago-kami-s1",
      "quote": "金屋子神社には神仏習合した火の神として三宝荒神の姿で描かれた掛け軸も残されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lukisan kuil menggambarkan dewa itu sebagai Sanbō Kōjin sinkretis.",
        "en": "A shrine painting portrays the deity as syncretic Sanbō Kōjin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanayago-kami-c03",
      "source_id": "kanayago-kami-s1",
      "quote": "しかし、自ら、元々西方に縁のある神であるとの理由で、白鷺に乗って、西方の出雲国能義郡黒田奥比田（現 金屋子神社の社地）の山林に着き、桂の木にて羽を休めていたところを宮司の祖先である安倍正重が発見し、長田兵部朝日長者が桂の木の横に神殿を建立したという（途中、吉備国中山にも立寄ったとの伝説がある）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda kuil menyebut dewa itu tiba di Izumo dengan bangau putih.",
        "en": "A shrine legend has the deity reach Izumo on a white heron."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanayago-kami-c04",
      "source_id": "kanayago-kami-s1",
      "quote": "各地で金屋子神は自ら村下（むらげ：鍛冶の技師長）となり、鍛冶の指導を行ったとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah setempat menyebut dewa itu mengajarkan pandai besi.",
        "en": "Local accounts credit the deity with teaching smithing."
      }
    }

      "id": "katakurokushin-c03",
      "source_id": "katakurokushin-s1",
      "quote": "最初に産まれたのは石土毘古神（いはつちびこのかみ）である。 == 石巣比売神 == 2番目が石巣比売神（いはすひめのかみ）である。 『古史伝』では、この二神は上筒男神の別名であるとしている。『神名考』では、石土毘古神は土を、石巣比売神は砂を司る神であるとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir mengaitkan Ishitsuchibiko dengan tanah dan Ishisuhime dengan pasir.",
        "en": "Interpretations associate Ishitsuchibiko with soil and Ishisuhime with sand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "katakurokushin-c04",
      "source_id": "katakurokushin-s1",
      "quote": "4番目は天之吹男神（あめのふきおのかみ）である。『古事記伝』では、その名前から「大祓詞」に登場する気吹戸主（いぶきどぬし）と同神と解釈している。『神名考』では屋上の神としている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ameno-fukio ditafsirkan melalui atap atau dewa penyucian angin.",
        "en": "Ameno-fukio is interpreted through the roof or wind-purifying deity."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "カヤナルミ（賀夜奈流美、加夜奈留美、賀屋鳴比女）とは、『延喜式』に収録されている祝詞である「出雲国造神賀詞」に登場する神。「出雲国造神賀詞」では賀夜奈流美命と表記され、飛鳥坐神社・加夜奈留美命神社では加夜奈留美命と表記されている。",

      "quote": "飛鳥坐神社・加夜奈留美命神社では女神としている。 == 概要 == 『古事記』『日本書紀』に登場せず、その出自、名義、伝説についてはいずれも不明。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kuil menganggap Kayanarumi perempuan, sedangkan asal-usul dan legendanya tetap kabur.",

    {
      "id": "kayanarumi-no-kami-c03",
      "source_id": "kayanarumi-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "「出雲国造神賀詞」には「賀夜奈流美命乃御魂乎飛鳥乃神奈備尓坐天皇孫命能近守神登貢置天」と記述されており、大物主櫛瓶玉命、阿遅須伎高孫根乃命、事代主ともに皇室を守護する神として述べられている。",

      "statement": {

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kazenbō adalah yōkai yang digambar dalam kumpulan Sekien. Ia tampak sebagai biksu pengemis berselubung api dan asap di Toribeyama. Satu kisah menjelaskannya sebagai biksu yang gagal mencapai Tanah Suci setelah pengorbanan api.",
      "en": "Kazenbō is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s collection. It appears as a beggar monk enveloped in fire and smoke at Toribeyama. One account explains it as a monk who failed to attain the Pure Land after fiery sacrifice.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kazenbo-c01",
        "kazenbo-c02",
        "kazenbo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teori lain menganggapnya ciptaan Sekien dari nama tempat di Edo. Kisah belakangan menyebut penampakan itu tidak berbahaya tetapi mengganggu. Kisah itu menyebutnya menderita dalam api yang tidak menghabiskannya lalu menghilang.",
      "en": "Another theory treats it as Sekien’s invention from an Edo place name. A later account calls the apparition harmless but disturbing. That account has it suffer in unconsuming flames before disappearing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kazenbo-c04",
        "kazenbo-c05",
        "kazenbo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c01",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s1",
      "quote": "火前坊（かぜんぼう）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔百鬼拾遺』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kazenbō adalah yōkai yang digambar dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
        "en": "Kazenbō is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c02",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s1",
      "quote": "平安時代頃に葬送地として知られた京都の鳥部山に現れるという妖怪で、画図では炎と煙に包まれた乞食坊主の姿で描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tampak sebagai biksu pengemis berselubung api dan asap di Toribeyama.",
        "en": "It appears as a beggar monk enveloped in fire and smoke at Toribeyama."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c03",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s1",
      "quote": "鳥部山は有力な皇族や貴族が葬られており、10世紀末頃には高僧たちがこの地で焚死往生を願って自らの体に火を放って命を絶ったといわれ、その信仰儀式を人目見ようとする庶民たちも多かったが、中には儀式に反し、現世に未練があるなどして極楽往生できなかった者もいたらしく、そうした僧の霊が僧形の怪火となって鳥部山に現れたものが火前坊とされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah menjelaskannya sebagai biksu yang gagal mencapai Tanah Suci setelah pengorbanan api.",
        "en": "One account explains it as a monk who failed to attain the Pure Land after fiery sacrifice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c04",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s1",
      "quote": "また、江戸麻布の地名「我善坊谷」から鳥山石燕が創作したものとする異説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teori lain menganggapnya ciptaan Sekien dari nama tempat di Edo.",
        "en": "Another theory treats it as Sekien’s invention from an Edo place name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c05",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s2",
      "quote": "They don’t do anything harmful, but their horrific appearance is very disturbing.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebut penampakan itu tidak berbahaya tetapi mengganggu.",
        "en": "A later account calls the apparition harmless but disturbing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kazenbo-c06",
      "source_id": "kazenbo-s2",
      "quote": "They materialize, appear to suffer in flames which never completely consume them, and then disappear.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menyebutnya menderita dalam api yang tidak menghabiskannya lalu menghilang.",
        "en": "That account has it suffer in unconsuming flames before disappearing."

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kokuri-baba adalah yōkai perempuan tua dalam kumpulan Sekien. Sekien menggambarkan janda pendeta yang mencuri persembahan kuil dan memakan mayat. Teks Tiongkok yang dikutip menjelaskan kata istri pendeta, bukan yōkai ini.",
      "en": "Kokuri-baba is an old-woman yōkai in Sekien’s collection. Sekien depicts a priest’s widow stealing temple offerings and eating corpses. The cited Chinese text explains the word priest’s wife, not this yōkai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kokuri-baba-c01",
        "kokuri-baba-c02",
        "kokuri-baba-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Peneliti menafsirkan gambar itu sebagai sindiran terhadap pendeta yang melanggar sumpah. Kisah belakangan menempatkannya tersembunyi di bagian belakang kuil sambil memakan mayat. Uraian belakangan secara tegas mengatribusikan penciptaannya kepada Sekien.",
      "en": "Researchers interpret the image as satire of priests breaking their vows. A later account places her hidden in temple quarters feeding on corpses. The later account explicitly attributes the invention to Sekien.",

        "kokuri-baba-c05",
        "kokuri-baba-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c01",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s1",
      "quote": "古庫裏婆（こくりばばあ）は、鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『今昔百鬼拾遺』にある日本の妖怪で、老婆のすがたをした妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kokuri-baba adalah yōkai perempuan tua dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
        "en": "Kokuri-baba is an old-woman yōkai in Sekien’s collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c02",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s1",
      "quote": "『今昔百鬼拾遺』では、猫をかたわらに寝かせて糸をよる作業をしている老婆のすがたで描かれており、石燕による解説文には、ある山寺の庫裏（くり）に7代も前の住職の梵嫂（ぼんそう、僧侶の妻・梵妻）が住み着いており、檀家が寺におさめた食べ物や金銭を盗み取ったり、墓地に葬られた屍を掘り起こし髪の毛を編んで着物とし皮をはいで死肉を喰らうようになったものだとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menggambarkan janda pendeta yang mencuri persembahan kuil dan memakan mayat.",
        "en": "Sekien depicts a priest’s widow stealing temple offerings and eating corpses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c03",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s1",
      "quote": "「僧の妻を梵嫂（ぼんさう）といえるよし 輟耕録（てつこうろく）に見えたり」と、文頭で石燕は「梵嫂」という言葉について述べているが、『輟耕録』（中国の明代の随筆。日本でも流布していた）に記されているのは梵嫂についてであり、古庫裏婆について記されているわけではない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Tiongkok yang dikutip menjelaskan kata istri pendeta, bukan yōkai ini.",
        "en": "The cited Chinese text explains the word priest’s wife, not this yōkai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c04",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s1",
      "quote": "石燕がこの絵の構図の元にしたのは鈴木春信『絵本花葛蘿』（明和元年刊）に描かれている老女の絵（同図はまた西川祐信が『絵本倭比事』（1742年）に描いた蜷川智菖の妻の絵を下敷きとしている）ではないかとの指摘もある他、僧侶の妻という単語をさしはさんでいる点から江戸時代の破戒僧を風刺した石燕による創作ではないかとも考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menafsirkan gambar itu sebagai sindiran terhadap pendeta yang melanggar sumpah.",
        "en": "Researchers interpret the image as satire of priests breaking their vows."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c05",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s2",
      "quote": "She hides herself away in the back of the temple and feeds off of human corpses.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menempatkannya tersembunyi di bagian belakang kuil sambil memakan mayat.",
        "en": "A later account places her hidden in temple quarters feeding on corpses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kokuri-baba-c06",
      "source_id": "kokuri-baba-s2",
      "quote": "Kokuri babā was invented by Toriyama Sekien for his book Konjaku hyakki shūi .",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan secara tegas mengatribusikan penciptaannya kepada Sekien.",
        "en": "The later account explicitly attributes the invention to Sekien."
      }
    }
  ],
